## 9. The hook set (milestone M6)

The source repository (102_3d_piter, commit `0568f7af`) hard-codes a handful of behaviours for its north into the engine modules: trees and grass tufts are kept out of the places that a landmark module draws, the Paradny
podium is a 3.3 m deck that `layout.heightAt` knows, the Tauride Garden has its own crowd cap, cars and pedestrians stand on the deck of a landmark service. M6 gives the North these behaviours as a **small set of hooks**:
the engine reads a table of the area module through `src/world/lib/area-tables.js` (section 3.1, rows `HOOKS` and `HEIGHT_PATCHES`) and does exactly what it did before when the key is absent, so **the strip is unchanged**
(`HOOKS = {}`, `HEIGHT_PATCHES = []`; proved by the strip oracles of 9.6). The North builds both in **one place**, `src/areas/north/hooks.js`; `area.js` only calls `makeHooks(MODULES)` and `makeHeightPatches(MODULES)` at its tail.
The engine edit is 10 files (T3 adds two call sites: `environment/ground.js` and `environment/field.js`; `layout.js`, `drive/handling.js`, `environment/trees.js`, `environment/lawns.js`, `props/world.js`, `streets.js`, `streets/parking.js`, `people.js`, `people/nav.js`, `people/sim.js`): 57 lines added,
13 replaced, comments included (plus the reader `lib/area-tables.js`, which validates both tables, 9.1); the rest is pure data and pure functions under `src/areas/north/`. The ids H01 .. H16 below are the items of the M6 inventory (one per North-specific engine edit of the source, traced through
its git history; the inventory itself is a working paper outside the repository, and the tables of 9.1 and 9.5 restate every item this section needs).

**What is ACTIVE in the generic North after M6** (it changes what the North builds or does today):

* the **Smolny, Suvorov and Kirochnaya-campus keep-outs**: the modules `landmarksSmolny`, `landmarksSuvorov` and `landmarksKirochnaya` are in the North's `MODULES` since M5, so the trees (and, in the campus, the grass tufts) are kept out of
  their regions (9.5): 19 trees fewer in the tree list (15 instances in `npm run check:north`), nothing else moves;
* the **Tauride Garden crowd cap** (`ZONES.peopleDensity.garden { factor 0.22, maxPeople 60 }`, `HOOKS.inGarden`): **the ONE hook of M6 that changes the generic North's crowd, and the only one that does not wait for a landmark
  module** (decision and evidence in 9.3). The garden is a plain OSM park in the generic North; without the cap it held 333 of the 638 visible people on average.

**What was DORMANT until the Tauride remake added `'landmarksTauride'` to `MODULES` (milestone M6 delivered it dormant; it is ACTIVE since T1, 8.9, and the words "dormant" in the rest of this section describe the M6 state)**: the whole Paradny set (the height patch, the tree / tuft / prop keep-out, the pedestrian-only ways, the parking reject) and the deck service
(`HOOKS.deckServices` lists the service; it is inert while the service is absent, and the module publishes it now). How the remake switched them on: 9.5; what it changed, measured: 8.9. A page without the module in its list is the old dormant North (the `dormant` child of `north-hooks-test`, the `dormant` variant of `north-keepout-test`).

### 9.1 The contract (frozen by the foundation commit)

`ctx.buildList` is the list of the modules this build really draws (`main.js`, `tools/check.mjs`); a missing list counts as "not built", as in the source (`ctx.buildList?.includes(name)`). `MODULES` is the page's list in `area.js`.

| Key | Signature | Gating | Read by (item) | Body in | Strip default |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `HEIGHT_PATCHES` | `[{ id: string, box: [x0, x1, z0, z1], at: (x, z) => number \| null }]`: per patch in array order a box reject (edges inclusive), then `at()`; the first non-null wins (0 is an answer); then the old waterway-bed / `GROUND_Y` line. Read at CALL time | `MODULES` (layout has no ctx): `[]` unless `landmarksTauride` is listed, then `[paradny, garden]` (T2: the garden relief, `landmarksTauride/height.js`, box `RELIEF_BOX` = the garden bbox, `null` where no hill lifts the ground) | `layout.js heightAt` (H01) | `hooks/height.js`, geometry `hooks/paradny.js` | `[]`: the old line |
| `HOOKS.treeCleared` | `(ctx) => ((x, z) => boolean) \| null`: soft tree clearing (`cleared()` runs only under `!o.force` and no caller of `placeTrees` forces, so mapped OSM trees are cleared too: none lies in the rectangles). **T5 fix round 1:** with `landmarksTauride` built also the palace parterre `PALACE_PARTERRE` (x 1356 .. 1436, z -1660 .. -1598 since the review fix, it was 1404 / -1606 and left the east wing behind 17 crowns of the wood: the lawn in front of the Tauride Palace's garden front, so the T11 pose sees the facade across the lake; the OSM wood w1274934137 of the palace side keeps its trees on both sides of it) | `landmarksSmolny` (ACTIVE), `landmarksTauride` (ACTIVE) | `environment/trees.js cleared()` (H02) | `hooks/keep-out.js` | absent: no extra clearing |
| `HOOKS.treeReject` | `(ctx) => ((x, z) => 'paradny' \| 'garden' \| 'nursery' \| 'campus' \| 'suvorov' \| null) \| null`: hard reject, mapped trees included; the string is the `ENV_DEBUG` reason (`green:suvorov`, `yard:campus`, `osm:garden` ...); first match in that order. **T5:** `'garden'` = inside the Tauride Garden outline (`hooks/people.js inGarden`) when `landmarksTauride` is built: the garden plants its own trees (8.14), the 7 mapped OSM trees among them (the facade's `TREES` carry no ids to claim; the procedural trees were already kept out by the claimed park and woods) | `landmarksTauride` (dormant), `landmarksKirochnaya` and `landmarksSuvorov` (ACTIVE) | `environment/trees.js plant()` (H03) | `hooks/keep-out.js` | absent |
| `HOOKS.tuftReject` | `(ctx) => ((x, z) => boolean) \| null`: no grass tufts (T8b: also in the garden nursery lot, `landmarksTauride/data/nursery.js inNursery`) | `landmarksTauride` (dormant), `landmarksKirochnaya` (ACTIVE) | `environment/lawns.js buildTufts()` (H04) | `hooks/keep-out.js` | absent |
| `HOOKS.propReject` | `(ctx) => ((cx, cz) => boolean) \| null`: no bench / bin / kiosk stamped (T8b: also in the garden nursery lot) | `landmarksTauride` (dormant) | `props/world.js fits()` (H05) | `hooks/keep-out.js` | absent |
| `HOOKS.pedestrianOnly` | `(ctx) => ((way) => boolean) \| null`; `way.rank`, `way.x[]`, `way.z[]`: rank 0 and MORE than 60 % of the points in the podium | `landmarksTauride` (dormant) | `streets.js build()` (H06) | `hooks/keep-out.js` | absent: `false` |
| `HOOKS.parkingReject` | `((x, z) => boolean) \| undefined`: a plain function, because `planParking(net)` has no ctx | `MODULES`, when `makeHooks(MODULES)` runs (dormant) | `streets/parking.js` (H07) | `hooks/keep-out.js` (`makeParkingReject`) | absent |
| `HOOKS.carKeepOut` (T2a) | `((x, z) => boolean) \| undefined`: the garden outline is CLOSED to cars (the ring of `hooks/people.js inGarden`: strict bbox, then the even-odd ring test; no exception for the service lane): a plain function like `parkingReject`, because the handling needs no ctx | `MODULES`, when `makeHooks(MODULES)` runs: undefined unless `landmarksTauride` is listed | `drive/handling.js` (`solidBlocked`: `if (KEEP_OUT && KEEP_OUT(x, z)) return true;`, read once per car; and in `sub()` the footprint count `keepPoints`: the undo of a contact push that grows it, the keep-out term of the embedded rule; T2 fix round 2) | `hooks/keep-out.js` (`makeCarKeepOut`) | absent: no keep-out |
| `HOOKS.inGarden` | `((x, z) => boolean) \| undefined`: the Tauride Garden polygon (OSM park r1582734), for the crowd cap; the numbers are the row `ZONES.peopleDensity.garden` of `area.js` | **none: ACTIVE in the generic North** (9.3) | `people/nav.js` (H08; it is the only people file that imports `HOOKS`: `people/sim.js` reads `nav.inGarden` / `nav.gardenCfg`, `people.js` reads the row `ZONES.peopleDensity.garden`) | `hooks/people.js` | absent: no cap (the neutral row `{ 1, Infinity }`) and no garden code at all (`nav.gardenActive` false, 9.5 H08) |
| `HOOKS.deckServices` | `['landmarksTauride']`: names of `ctx.services` whose `deckY(x, z)` is consulted FIRST (cars and pedestrians); a service that is absent, has no `deckY` or answers `null` / `undefined` is skipped (the old chain answers; `drive/handling.js` skips a non-finite number too, `nav.surfaceY` ignores a non-finite deck itself), `0` is a deck. **T4b:** a point on such a deck is NOT water for the pedestrians (`people/nav.js water()`: the garden bridges cross the ponds, which are in the field's water mask), and a service may publish `deckLift(x, z) -> number \| null` = what the walkers' feet stand above its deck (`nav.surfaceY`: absent / null = the engine's pavement lift 0.12; the garden bridges answer `WALK_LIFT` 0.02, the Paradny podium null) | none (inert while the service is absent) | `drive/handling.js`, `people/nav.js water() / surfaceY` (H09) | `hooks.js`; the service in `src/world/landmarksTauride.js` (`courts.deckY ?? bridges.deckY`, `deckLift`) | absent: the old chain |
| `HOOKS.doorServices` (T7) | `['landmarksNorthFull', 'landmarksOktyabrsky']`: names of `ctx.services` whose `entrances` (`{ x, z, rotY, bldg }`, the door points of `lib/door-points.js`) are pedestrian doors besides the city's; a point without a finite `rotY` (a clearing disc) is skipped, a service that is absent or has no `entrances` array is skipped | none (a constant array of the North; the strip's HOOKS is `{}`) | `people/nav.js` (the entrances block: after the city doors, the same `inPlay` / `zoneW > 0.3` test; `nav.stats.landmarkDoors`) | `hooks.js` (`DOOR_SERVICES`), validated in `lib/area-tables.js checkHooks` (an array of strings, else dropped with one warning) | absent / `[]`: the nav has the city's doors only (the strip) |
| `HOOKS.groundHoles` (T3a) | `(() => [flat ring, ...]) \| undefined`: the rings that are CUT OUT of the environment ground mesh like a water body or a metro shaft (triangulated into convex cuts, `clip.js triangulateRing`), so that the ground shader does not run under a floor that a landmark module draws itself. The ring is the garden outline (`hooks/people.js GARDEN_RING`) moved in by `GROUND_HOLE_INSET` = 6 cm (`hooks/ground.js insetRing`): the module's floor is the exact outline polygon, so the two meshes overlap by 6 cm all round (the floor is lifted 5 mm and wins the overlap by geometry; no crack shows between them). **T8b:** the second ring is the garden nursery lot (`landmarksTauride/data/nursery.js LOT_RING`, which shares the garden's west edge exactly) moved in by the same 6 cm: two rings now. A fresh copy of each ring on every call | `MODULES`, when `makeHooks(MODULES)` runs: undefined unless `landmarksTauride` is listed | `environment/ground.js groundCuts()` (`const rings = HOOKS.groundHoles ? HOOKS.groundHoles() : null;`, read once per build) | `hooks/ground.js` (`makeGroundHoles`) | absent: the ground is not cut |
| `HOOKS.groundWayReject` (T3b) | `((road) => boolean) \| undefined`: a road record of the facade (`{ id, ... }`) that the ground CLASS MASK does not paint (the grey paving / gravel paint of footways and paths, `field.js buildField`): the 120 garden ways (`hooks/ground.js GARDEN_WAY_IDS`, the ids of `data/paths.js`, equal to the claim of the sub-group `tauride-garden`). A plain function, gated by the module list | `MODULES`, when `makeHooks(MODULES)` runs: undefined unless `landmarksTauride` is listed | `environment/field.js buildField()` (`const wayReject = HOOKS.groundWayReject;` and `if (wayReject && wayReject(r)) continue;` in the footway paint loop) | `hooks/ground.js` (`makeGroundWayReject`) | absent: every way is painted |
| `HOOKS.noLanding` (T4a fix 1) | **REMOVED (section 13.7: a landing is never refused on water; HOOKS has fifteen keys).** It was `((x, z) => boolean) \| undefined`: where a flight landing is refused as 'water' although the ground is not below -1 m. `core/player.js` has the field `noLanding` (default null) and `landingBlocked()` returns 'water' when it answers true; `src/main.js` sets `player.noLanding = HOOKS.noLanding \|\| null` (read through `lib/area-tables.js`, validated there as a function). North: `hooks/water.js makeNoLanding(MODULES)`: true where `gardenHeight` (the terrain) is at or below `COLLIDER.contourY` (-0.3: the water-side face of the shore colliders), `undefined` unless MODULES has `'landmarksTauride'`; the strip exports no HOOKS, so its player is the same |
| `HOOKS.waterBodies` (T4a) | `(() => [{ id, o, h }]) \| undefined`: the water bodies that a landmark module draws itself, as flat rings (outer ring `o`, island holes `h`), rasterised into the field's WATER mask right after the river polygons (`environment/field.js buildField`: `for (const w of HOOKS.waterBodies ? HOOKS.waterBodies() : []) fillPoly(dg, wat, ...)`). `env.waterAt` / `env.kindAt` (both read `field.shore`), the distance atlas of the water shader (shallow tint, tide line, the duckweed band of the Small Pond), the trees, the wildlife and the pedestrians' `nav.blocked` then know the ponds. **Not `env.shoreDist`** (T4b correction of the T4a row: `environment.js` sets it to `layout.shoreDist`, the waterways only; the garden's own exact distance is `ctx.services.landmarksTauride.ponds.shoreDist`). North: `hooks/water.js makeWaterBodies(MODULES)` returns the 136-vertex union of `landmarksTauride/data/water.js` with its 3 island holes, `undefined` unless MODULES has `'landmarksTauride'`; the strip exports no HOOKS, so its field is the same bytes (`field-dump`: sha1 equal on a 4 m lattice of 12 quantities; the North field differs only in `shore` (4,227 lattice points) and `free()` (48) inside x 1032 .. 1480, z -1731 .. -1267) |
| `HOOKS.duckPonds` (T4b) | `(() => { y, offset, margin, rings, keepOut, shoreDist, inWater }) \| undefined`: duck lanes on the ponds of a landmark module (`wildlife/ducks.js`: a pond branch beside the waterway lanes of `LANE_KEYS`): `rings` = the shore lines as `[[x, z], ...]` with the water on the LEFT of travel (as the bank chains of the waterways have it), a lane is `offset` m into the water, every lane sample must be water for the field (`env.waterAt`, as on the canals) AND at least `margin` m from every shore by the hook's exact `shoreDist`, with 2.5 m of free water beyond; `keepOut` = `[{ x, z, r }]` the six bridge decks + the dam (no lane under a deck); `y` = the lane level (`data/ponds.js WATER_Y`; the waterway lanes carry no `y` and keep `L.WATERWAYS[key].water`). Lane key `'pond'` (tools/wildlife-test.mjs allows it). North: `hooks/water.js makeDuckPonds(MODULES)`, `undefined` unless MODULES has `'landmarksTauride'`; the strip exports no HOOKS, so its ducks are the same (`wildlife-test`, the strip signature) | `MODULES` (dormant) | `wildlife/ducks.js buildDucks()` | `hooks/water.js` | absent: the waterway lanes only |
| `HOOKS.deckUnderside` (T8a) | `((poly) => boolean) \| undefined`: `city/roofs.js addDeck` asks it for every flat deck polygon `{ o, h }`; true = the deck also gets a DOWN-facing cap 1 cm under it in 0.6 of the roof colour (the deck is a one-sided face and the outline pass is double-sided: a viewer below the eaves of a low building saw the sky through its roof and the deck outline floating in the air). `hooks/decks.js makeDeckUnderside`: the 13 footprints of `data/footprints.js` (matched by the first vertex) and any deck centred in the garden outline; `undefined` unless `MODULES` has `landmarksTauride`; the strip has no `HOOKS`: its decks are the same bytes. +70 triangles in `city-roofs` of the North. |

Rules of the contract:

* The engine reads the hooks ONLY through `src/world/lib/area-tables.js`, never from `area.js`; `layout.js` gains no export (`tools/layout-golden.mjs` lists every export). The shape of the call site is `const f = HOOKS.key?.(ctx);` once per build,
  then `if (f && f(x, z))` per candidate (the tuft / prop / tree paths are hot), about 3 lines per site plus one import.
* Every `(ctx)` factory is evaluated ONCE per build and returns `null` when none of the modules it serves is in `ctx.buildList`.
* A key that is absent means the engine does exactly what it does today. The files under `src/areas/north/hooks/` are PURE DATA and pure functions: no three.js, no import of `layout.js`, **no import of a data facade** (`tools/areas-test.mjs`
  loads the area module without the import-map remap and would get the strip's facade); the coordinates are static (`environment` is built first, so it cannot read the service of a landmark module).
* The hooks that serve a landmark module are **dormant until the module is in `MODULES`**, except the crowd cap (`inGarden` and its row are gated by nothing, 9.3).
* **The tables are validated** by `lib/area-tables.js` (`checkHooks`, `checkPatches`), so that the table of a hand-written area cannot crash the build in an unnamed place: a known `HOOKS` key that is not a function (`false` is the easy way to "disable" a hook, and
  `false?.()` throws), a `deckServices` that is not an array of strings, a `HEIGHT_PATCHES` entry that is not `{ id: string, box: [4 finite numbers], at: function }`, or a table of the wrong kind, is dropped with ONE `console.warn` that names the key or the patch
  (`undefined` / `null` mean "none" and are silent; unknown keys pass). A well-formed table is handed out as it is (the strip's `{}` and `[]` are the same objects), so the engine, the strip's behaviour and the tests that push into the live list see no difference. What a factory
  RETURNS is not checked (it needs a `ctx`).
* `HEIGHT_PATCHES` is read at CALL time (`area-tables.js` hands out the area module's own array when it is well-formed), so a test or the remake can push into it; `heightAt` is evaluated for the whole map at start-up, hence the box reject comes first and the loop
  is an indexed one (a `for...of` cost ~6 ms of the 66 ms cold pass over 82,500 calls).

`npm run hooks:all` = `hooks:syntax` + `hooks` + `hooks:height` + `hooks:keepout` + `hooks:people`. The CI job `verify` has no `npm ci` on purpose, so it runs only what imports no three.js (both layout goldens, `hooks-syntax-check`, `north-hooks-test`, `north-keepout-test --only pure`, the
fast part of `north-people-hooks-test`; each was run in a copy of the tree without `node_modules`); the three engine parts (`hooks:height`, `hooks:keepout`, `north-people-hooks-test --scene`) run locally. **The engine call sites (trees, tufts, props, ways, parking, `heightAt`, handling, nav, sim) are therefore
exercised ONLY by the local `npm run hooks:all` (it needs `npm ci`)**: the CI steps prove that the engine files parse (`node --check`), that the area side is right (contract, golden, tables) and, as TEXT, that each engine file still reads its hook through `lib/area-tables.js` (group `static` of `north-hooks-test`); a call site whose logic
was deleted passes all of them. Until the committed bake of M8 even those steps are not reached in a real run: the first step of the job, `bake-manifest-check`, is red on purpose (9.6).

`hooks/paradny.js` is a port of `src/world/landmarksTauride/paradny-surface.js` of the source (commit `0568f7af`): the same expressions and values (the deck answered identically on 641,504 probe points, the flights, landings and box are
equal to the source module's), with the podium ring (14 vertices, bbox x 741 .. 1055, z -1205 .. -873, 74,115 m2) and the STEPS polylines (ways w1266791536, w1266791538, w1303266326 spliced into w1266791538, w1266791542, w1266791545,
w1266791548, w1266791551, w1266791557, w1266791560) embedded as literals; `PARADNY_BOX` is `[741, 1058.51, -1205, -873]` (the stairs reach x 1057.6, plus half their width). The literals equal the STEPS records of the North facade, in the order
of the data.

### 9.3 Decisions taken

* The Paradny set is delivered DORMANT (the Tauride remake adds `landmarksTauride` to the North `MODULES`).
* The Paradny geometry lives in `src/areas/north/hooks/paradny.js` (a port, not a second copy of `landmarksTauride/paradny-surface.js` in `src/world`, which the remake owns).
* The Kirochnaya `CAMPUS` polygon (8 points) is EMBEDDED in `hooks/keep-out.js` with an equality test against `src/world/landmarksKirochnaya/groups.js` (and `inCampus` answers the same on 200,000 points). The copy exists because the HOOKS
  files import no data facade (that file imports `../data/city.js` for `CAMPUS_ROADS`); `area.js` itself imports the groups files since M5 (the Kirochnaya one derives its `osm` list from `ROADS` of the facade, so the area module is transitively facade-dependent and, loaded without the remap as `areas-test` does, sees the strip's facade there), so the rule binds `hooks.js` and `hooks/*.js`, whose answers must not depend on the remap, and not the whole area module.
* The polygon of `inGarden` is the OSM park r1582734 ring, embedded (the 66 vertices of the North facade record, one ring, no holes), with an equality test against the facade `GREEN` record. The source's own `TAURIDE_GARDEN` has 81 vertices (the
  closing one included, opposite winding): 14 of them are within 0.35 m of a straight edge of the facade ring (the farthest: 0.3415 m), the areas are 197,570 and 197,559 m2 and 142 of 1,344,321 probes of a 0.5 m grid differ.
* Cars climb the Paradny podium only through a deck service (the source behaviour: `handling.js` `deckServices`).
* The pond machinery (pond cuts, pond water mask, pond bed, removal of the thinning hack of `environment/trees.js`, garden bridges) belongs to the Tauride remake, not to M6; the pond bed will be a THIRD entry of `HEIGHT_PATCHES` (T2 made the garden relief the second),
  appended after the Paradny one in `hooks.js makeHeightPatches`.
* The Liteyny Bridge road stub (section 7) is a known gap, not an M6 hook.
* **The garden crowd cap is ACTIVE in the generic North** (decision of the integration step, reviewed against measurements; it replaces cluster C's variant in which the row existed only with `landmarksTauride`). Reasons: the frozen
  contract says `inGarden` is gated by nothing; the inventory of the source's edits (item H08) marks the cap ACTIVE in the generic North and as the one exception to "the generic North is bit-identical before / after M6"; and without it the generic North is visibly unbalanced: the garden
  (197,570 m2, beside the Tauride gate) takes more than half of the visible pedestrians. Evidence (CPU, `people-test --at tauride`, the camera at the Tauride gate (1226, -1268), 10 minutes, heads inside the ring
  of r1582734, `garden-crowd.mjs` of the baselines):

  | | mean | max | per minute | visible in the play area |
  |---|---:|---:|---|---:|
  | before M6 (clean `feat/areas` = the foundation baseline) | 348 | 394 | 215 281 353 371 382 384 368 369 378 380 | 638 |
  | merged tree, row removed in a scratch copy | 333 | 403 | 201 281 322 332 352 373 364 351 370 385 | 638 |
  | merged tree, row active (this commit) | **125** | **179** | 66 96 91 108 120 142 149 146 162 171 | 641 |

  The cap does what it should and no more: the visible population stays (638 -> 641, the people move to the streets), nothing outside the garden is thinned (scene test: 602 / 600 / 593 / 601 visible with the row, the head cap alone,
  the factor alone and the neutral row), and with the camera in the middle of the garden (3 minutes, `north-people-hooks-test --scene`) the row gives 76 / 91 against 403 / 454. **The cap is SOFT, as in the source**
  (`sim.js crowdedAt`: nobody picks a target or is re-placed in a full garden, but walkers on an already planned route and the scripted people are not refused), so the count creeps up during a run (66 -> 171) and the oracle of cluster E,
  "at most 60 heads", is not met and cannot be; the measurement is recorded, not graded. The cost to the other oracles: `npm run check:north` shows `people` 72 -> 71 meshes (one part type has no instance in the starting composition
  that the changed weights produce); with the row removed `people` is identical to the baseline. The verdicts of `people-test` are NOT evidence either way: its jump and carriageway counters move by +-45 jumps / by a factor of
  ten for any change of the nav lattice or the crowd (table in 9.6). To turn the cap off again: set `garden: undefined` in `ZONES.peopleDensity` of `src/areas/north/area.js` (the engine falls back to the neutral row) or gate it by
  `MODULES.includes('landmarksTauride')` as cluster C had done; `inGarden` and the engine need no change.

### 9.4 Clusters and the merge

* **F, foundation (the first commit, serial)**: the contract, `hooks.js`, `hooks/paradny.js`, the three stubs, the tail of `area.js`, the skeleton of this section.
* **A, terrain and decks**: `layout.js heightAt` (H01), `drive/handling.js` (H09, cars), `hooks/height.js`, `tools/north-height-test.mjs`.
* **B, keep-outs**: `environment/trees.js` (H02, H03), `environment/lawns.js` (H04), `props/world.js` (H05), `streets.js` (H06), `streets/parking.js` (H07), `hooks/keep-out.js`, `tools/north-keepout-test.mjs`.
* **C, people**: `people.js`, `people/nav.js`, `people/sim.js` (H08, and H09 for pedestrians), `hooks/people.js`, one data line in `area.js` (`ZONES.peopleDensity.garden`), `tools/north-people-hooks-test.mjs`.
* **E, oracles and baselines**: `tools/north-hooks-test.mjs`, `tools/north-env-snapshot.mjs`, `tools/golden/layout-north.json`, the area switch of `tools/layout-golden.mjs`, and the baselines of the strip and the North taken at the foundation
  commit (outside the repository: `D:/git2/n4spb-work/m6-baselines`, with `take.sh` / `compare.mjs`, equal to the clean `feat/areas` for every oracle).
* **Integration** (`m6-hooks`): the five branches merged with `git merge --no-ff` in the order foundation, A, B, C, E, without a conflict (the foundation edits the tail of `area.js`, cluster C one line in its `ZONES` block, `docs/AREAS.md`
  was touched by the foundation only). The integration step then (1) made the garden cap unconditional (9.3), (2) fixed the stale comments of `lib/area-tables.js`, the strip's `area.js`, `people.js` and `hooks.js`, (3) added the new tests to
  `package.json` and the CI, (4) re-took every oracle of 9.6 on the merged tree, (5) wrote this section and corrected 3.1, 5, 7 and 8.

Numbers of the North (real `MODULES`: Smolny, Suvorov, campus active, Paradny dormant), measured by the oracles of 9.6:

* **Keep-out regions** (verified against the geometry of the M5 modules by `tools/north-keepout-test.mjs --footprints`; the numbers are the source's, kept):
  * Smolny approach 1, x 2320 .. 2440, z -1847 .. -1820 (centre z -1833.5 = the cathedral door z): contains the entrance (2425, -1833) and the photo spot (2335, -1833); the only solids of the module in it are the 7 apse walls at x >= 2427.8
    (the cathedral footprint r2864743 starts at x 2428.3, so the east 12 m, 248 m2, of the rectangle lie inside it: harmless); the open part x 2320 .. 2428 holds none.
  * Smolny approach 2, x 2400 .. 2520, z -1553 .. -1533: its z centre -1543 is 1.5 m off the portico axis (-1544.49), its east 6 m (120 m2) overlap the Institute footprint, the only solid of the module in it is the 32.8 m facade wall at x 2513.1 +.
    It covers the last 120 m of the mapped allee only (the footway w1069307382 runs from x 2260).
  * Suvorov strip, x 1384 .. 1442, z -1226 .. -1208: the museum footprint is x 1384.3 .. 1441.2 (margins 0.3 / 0.8 m), its northmost wall z -1224.9 (1.1 m inside the north edge), the colliders of the module x 1383.8 .. 1441.7, northmost z -1222.2.
    The strip is the north 17 m of a 30.7 m deep building (330 of 1,044 m2 are footprint): the forecourts between the wings and the doorstep are kept clear, the rear half is not.
  * Kirochnaya campus polygon (8 points): 18 of the 20 group rings lie wholly inside, r3127701 (10 of 16 vertices) and the chapel r3127704 (4 of 8) straddle the edge; the 12 of 255 colliders of the module outside the polygon all belong to those two
    (none more than 8 m outside); the two gates (1230, -1244) and (1312, -1238) stand on the border (within the 5 m clearance). Three generic buildings have their centroid inside it (w232918731, r3127706, r17248223): vegetation is kept out around
    them too, as in the source.
  * No mapped OSM tree node and no tree row lies in any region (so "soft clearing also takes mapped trees" changes nothing today).
* **Trees** (environment snapshot, the page's full list): 10,903 -> 10,884 (-19; 26 added, 45 removed, 1 moved; `ENV_DEBUG` counters `green:cleared` 129 -> 586, `green:suvorov` 0 -> 11, `yard:suvorov` 0 -> 1, `yard:campus` 0 -> 3). Of the 72 changes none lies outside the allowed
  regions (the four rectangles and the campus grown by 8 m) except 38 that are the CASCADE of a random stream: the trees of a GREEN polygon or a courtyard come from ONE random stream (`trees.js seed()`), so a candidate that a hook rejects
  shifts the rest of ITS record (the source does the same), and the two parks that overlap the regions (GREEN w232918774 around the Suvorov Museum and the campus, GREEN r1948607 the Smolny park) re-roll up to 155 m from a region.
  So "every difference lies inside a keep-out region" is true for the records that touch a region and false for the cascade; the oracles grade exactly that (`--allow active`: no tree inside a region, every other change in a record that overlaps one).
  The environment test of the module (`north-keepout-test`, other counting) reads 11,263 -> 11,244; of its 28 trees that stood in the regions (Smolny 15 + 3, Suvorov 7, campus 3) none remains.
* **Tufts** (the pool is camera-centred; probe spots): the campus pool is empty (`campus-n` 154 -> 0, `campus-edge` 300 -> 1), the tuft cells of the 36 m lattice 2,814 -> 2,790 (41 differ, all inside the allowed regions), `suvorov`, `smolny-a/b`,
  `garden`, `podium`, `hero` and `control` unchanged.
* **Everything else identical**: streets (590,243 triangles, +0), lane graph 10,499, parked slots 2,448, bay lines 1,161, pedestrian-only ways 13, lamps 1,042, every props list (1,854 props in 28 types), the layout golden of the North, traffic.
  `npm run check:north`: 12 of 14 modules identical, `environment` 318,344 -> 318,314 triangles (-30), 13,879 -> 13,864 instances (-15), 9,439 -> 9,424 colliders (-15), and `people` 72 -> 71 meshes (the cap, 9.3).
* **Paradny (dormant)**, with `landmarksTauride` forced into the lists (what-if snapshot against the normal one of the same tree; the module itself is not built): trees 10,884 -> 10,877 (101 rejections: courtyard 91, green 7, yard 3), tuft
  cells 2,790 -> 2,747 and the 267 tufts of the podium probe -> 0, pedestrian-only ways 13 -> 69 (the 56 rank-0 ways with more than 60 % of their points in the podium), lanes 10,499 -> 10,289, parked slots 2,448 -> 2,447 and
  bay lines 1,161 -> 1,159 (cluster B's forced run; the snapshot's `--also` cannot switch the `MODULES`-gated parking reject), props inside the podium ring 6 -> 1 (the one left is a mapped bench: `props/osm.js` does not go through `fits()`, as in the source); the single random stream of the
  props re-rolls benches, urns and pots map-wide (1,854 -> 1,860 props): the hook changes props everywhere, which is why it must stay dormant until the module is there. **T1 (the module built and listed): the real activation reproduces every figure of this what-if inside the podium and on the streets (tufts 267 -> 0, pedestrian-only ways 13 -> 69, lanes 10,499 -> 10,289, parked slots 2,448 -> 2,447, bay lines 1,161 -> 1,159, podium ring 0 trees / 1 mapped prop / 0 tufts), but the tree total is 10,893 and the props 1,872, not 10,877 / 1,860: the CLAIMS of the module (not the hooks) re-roll the courtyards and the random streams; the table and the decomposition are in 8.9.**
* **Height patch** (patch listed): box [741, 1058.51, -1205, -873]; of the 105,894 points of the box on a 1 m lattice 74,389 are deck (74,247 plateau at 3.3, the rest the 8 stairs: flights 1.8 .. 4.3 m long (after the 0.9 m cut at each landing), gradient 0.43 .. 0.82 m/m, steepest pitch
  1.18 m/m with the landing step, top landing 3.315 = the source's +0.015) and 31,505 are the old line. Cost with the empty list: 66.2 ms against 66.3 ms (median of 14 cold runs, 82,500 calls); with the patch, the worst case (the whole podium
  box) 19.5 .. 21.9 ms against 2.5 .. 2.6 ms warm. Cars: through `deckServices` the real `createHandling` car climbs the first flight of each of the 8 stairs (ride error <= 0.09 m); without the service it stays at 0.03.
* **Garden crowd**: 9.3 (the Tauride gate, 10 minutes: mean 125 / max 179 heads against 333 / 403 without the row; the camera in the middle of the garden, 3 minutes: 76 / 91 against 403 / 454).

How the Tauride remake activates the Paradny set (checklist):

1. Add `'landmarksTauride'` to `MODULES` in `src/areas/north/area.js` (after `landmarksKirochnaya`; `PRUNE_SERVICES` too if the module publishes `entrances`). That alone switches on `HEIGHT_PATCHES` (`makeHeightPatches`), the parking reject
   (`makeHooks`) and, through `ctx.buildList`, the tree / tuft / prop / pedestrian-only hooks. The oracles that turn into "fires in the podium" checks: `north-keepout-test` (its `real` vs `off` assertions on streets / props are the dormant case),
   `north-height-test` (the patch is then listed, not pushed), `north-hooks-test` (the gating group), the North baselines of `m6-baselines` (retake them).
2. The module publishes `ctx.services.landmarksTauride.deckY(x, z)` (the podium and the garden bridges); cars and pedestrians then stand on it. It must also (a) register the stair ramps and landings with the physics (`ctx.physics.addStairs` per flight,
   `addWalkBox` per landing, as `landmarksTauride/paradny-courts.js:38,42` of the source): `heightAt` alone is not a sticky floor for the player, climbing is exact but a run down the steepest flight hops by up to 1.8 m;
   (b) put parapet colliders on the edge of the podium: `deckY` answers 3.3 for ANY podium point, so a car whose centre crosses the edge from the street would snap onto the deck; without the service (a dev subset `?only=` with the module in `MODULES`)
   the car ignores terrain above 0.45 m and sinks into the podium, as in the source.
3. Append the pond bed (-1.4 m) after the Paradny entry in `hooks.js makeHeightPatches`; add the garden bridges to the deck service; make the nav lattice walkable on the podium and the bridges (`nav.js`, 9.7).
4. If the module keeps its own copy of `paradny-surface.js` (it reads `STEPS` from the facade), add an equality test between that copy and `hooks/paradny.js` next to the existing ones.
5. Views: Digit4 (915, -1010) stands on the podium (ground 3.30): `tools/views-check.mjs` fails its ground band [-0.5, 1.5] with the patch active (the pose itself is walkable: the real `Player` walks 5 s in four directions from it). Digit5 stands on a
   bridge and Digit6 in the lake of the garden: move the three with the remake, or let the check accept `heightAt` where a patch answers.
6. The people simulation: the garden row stays as it is (it is already active); when the garden gets bridges, the cap's ring and the bridges' walkable cells must agree.

**Status after T1** (8.9): points 1, 2, 4 and 5 are done (the module is listed and publishes the deck; `paradny-courts.js` registers the 8 stair ramps with `addStairs`, the landings with `addWalkBox`, the podium walls with `addBox`; the equality test is the `paradny twin` group; Digit4 / 5 / 6 moved, Digit0 / 3 too). Open: point 2(b) is only half true (the walls stop a walker or a car coming from the street, not one leaving the deck: 8.9 "Known behaviour of the source"), points 3 and 6 belong to T2-T7.

### 9.7 Known gaps

* **The Paradny set and the deck service WERE untested against the real module (M6); since T1 they are** (8.9): the real module is built, headless, by `tools/tauride/paradny-walk.mjs` (8 stairs, 0 failing, with the real `Player`, the module's `addStairs` ramps and walls) and, since T1 fix 2, by section 8 of `tools/north-height-test.mjs`, which publishes nothing of its own: the car checks run through the module's real `ctx.services.landmarksTauride`, and `deckY` and `paradny.deckY` are compared with `hooks/paradny.js paradnyDeckY` bit for bit on 449,360 lattice points (0.5 m, box + 5 m; also against `heightAt`, and the service key against `HOOKS.deckServices`; the mutants `deckY: () => null`, a deck shifted by 0.5 m, the service key renamed, `paradny` dropped and `undefined` for `null` each fail). `north-keepout-test`'s `real` variant does NOT build the module (its hooks read the list; the module is only in the build list for its claims, see its header), so it is no proof of the service; before T1 fix 2 section 8 used a stand-in `{ deckY: paradnyDeckY }` and a module whose service answered null passed every gate; the car on the deck service with the module's own colliders is probed only by a scratch run (8.9 "Known behaviour of the source": the podium parapet does not stop a walker or a car leaving the deck, as in the source).
  The remake still owes the second height patch (the pond bed) and the garden bridges (9.5 point 3).
* **Pedestrians and the podium / bridges**: the nav lattice (`people/nav.js`) knows neither the podium nor the garden bridges; nobody paths onto them yet (the remake's, point 3 of the checklist in 9.5). `surfaceY` already stands a walker on the service deck.
* **The pond machinery** (pond cuts, pond water mask, pond bed, removal of the thinning hack of `trees.js`, garden bridges) is the remake's, not M6's. The Liteyny Bridge road stub (section 7) is no hook.
* **The crowd cap is soft** (9.3): the garden count creeps to ~170 within 10 minutes at the gate; a hard cap would need the walkers on planned routes to be refused too (a source behaviour kept). The ring is the facade's (66 vertices); the source's
  81-vertex ring differs on 0.01 % of a 0.5 m grid.
* **Cascades**: the random stream of a GREEN polygon / courtyard (trees: 38 changes outside the +8 m boxes; 72 changes in total) and the single stream of the props (a Paradny prop hook changes about 400 prop rows map-wide once
  `landmarksTauride` is built). Both are the source's behaviour. The oracles grade them as "(cascade)".
* **Mapped OSM furniture** is not covered by `propReject`: `props/osm.js` places benches / bins / racks with `okPoint()`, not with `fits()`, and the source hook sits in `fits()` only (one mapped bench stays on the podium, as in the source).
* **`treeCleared` applies to mapped OSM trees** (no caller of `placeTrees` passes `force`); none lies in the rectangles, the strip or the campus today. T5 fix round 1: the parterre rectangle takes 28 trees of the wood w1274934137 (`wood:cleared`); no mapped node stands in it.
* **The Smolny rectangles are the source's numbers**, not the module's geometry: approach 2 is 1.5 m off the portico axis and 6 m of it lie inside the Institute; approach 1 covers 12 m inside the cathedral; the second covers only the last 120 m of the allee;
  the Suvorov strip is the north 17 m of the museum's 30.7 m. The Kirochnaya campus polygon cuts the buildings r3127701 and r3127704.
* **`tools/city-signature.mjs` cannot run as the North** (it reads `src/world/data/<region>` directly): there is no North city signature; the North's city is covered by `check:north` and the claims gates.
* **Not run (need a GPU or a browser)** (M7: `nav-bake-equiv --area north` exists and prints a clear FAIL line until a scratch North bake is made, 10.5; the browser flows of the door gate and the place pick are listed in 10.6): `drive-test`, `drive-garage-test`, `nav-bake-equiv`, `baked-check`, the bake byte comparison, the screenshots. `signals-knock-test` exits 1 on the clean `feat/areas` too. The bake is stale (every `src` file
  changes the global revision) until the committed bake of M8; the nav bake is not affected by the garden row (the graph uses the raw zone weights; a node check of `nav-bake-equiv-page.js` passed with the row).
* **CI** (M7: a second job `engine` with `npm ci` now runs `hooks:all`, the Tauride tests, the claims draw audit, `cross-places-test` and `north-knock-test`; it does not gate `deploy` yet, 5.3 and 10.5): `hooks:height`, `hooks:keepout` (engine part) and `north-people-hooks-test --scene` need three.js and run locally with `npm run hooks:all`; the `verify` job has no `npm ci` on purpose, so the engine call sites are exercised only locally (9.2) and the CI steps stay green when a call site
  is deleted. The optional source-equality parts of `north-hooks-test` (`--source <dir>`, else `PITER_SRC`, else `D:/git2/agi-fun-tests/102_3d_piter`) and of `north-people-hooks-test` (`PITER_SRC`, else the same default) skip themselves when the source repository is absent; the Paradny geometry is pinned
  without it by `tools/golden/paradny-deck.json`.

## 10. M7 preparation (gates, NorthFull polish, the missing landmarks, tools)

Milestone M7 "integration" was prepared in four parallel clusters off `feat/areas` (3d999e8, the T4a tree; `feat/areas` moved to 4113f2f, the T4b merge, while they ran) and merged on `m7-prep` with `git merge --no-ff` in the
order A `m7-gates`, B `m7-northfull`, C `m7-landmarks`, D `m7-tools`: **no textual conflict** (the foreseen ones did not materialise: cluster A appends a `CROSS_PLACES` tail to `src/areas/north/area.js` while cluster C edits its
module rows and imports; clusters B and C both edit `src/world/landmarksOktyabrsky.js` in different hunks (the door points vs the monument part); cluster D alone edits `tools/people-test.mjs`, `package.json` and `docs/AREAS.md`).
The integration then (1) wired what the clusters could not touch (10.4), (2) re-took every oracle on the merged tree (10.6), (3) wrote this section and restated 3 (the gate bullet of 7), 5, 7, 8.2, 8.7 and 9.7. The Tauride branch
`tb-t4b` was being built at the same time: none of its files (`landmarksTauride*`, `north/hooks*`, `people/nav.js`, `wildlife/ducks.js`, `environment/water.js`, `tools/tauride*`) is touched by M7.

### 10.1 A, gates: no prompt indoors, the door gate G3, the places of the other area

* **No prompt indoors**. The gate machine (`core/gates.js`) takes an `indoors` input, treated like `blocked` (no chip, no question) but named, so the test says what it proves; `needExit` and the cooldown keep running, so stepping
  out re-arms the gate at once. `createGates` reads it through `deps.isIndoors`, default `() => !!ctx.indoor.zone`: `core/zones.js` sets `ctx.indoor.zone` to the def of the zone that holds the CAMERA every frame (the walk-in interiors
  register one with `ctx.zones.add`: the bookstore `podpisnye-shop`, the Moscow station interiors, the Kazan interior, the metro vestibules; the auto-underground zone when the camera is 0.6 m below the terrain). Proven at the machine
  level, the DOM level and against the real bookstore zone of `landmarksNorthFull` (middle (-204.9, -227.8): no chip, no offer; the street in front at the same depth: both). The strip's gates are unchanged: no registered zone lies within
  g1's chip zone (z -405 .. -315, x -420 .. 835; the bookstore interior ends 66 m south of it, the station interiors are at z ~300, the Kazan interior at x -1500, the metro halls are underground and blocked anyway); `gates-test` pins g1 / g0
  with no door and `isIndoors` false.
* **The door gate G3** (`src/areas/registry.js`): `{ id 'g3', kind 'door', from north, to strip, at (461.05, -1350.15) = the middle of the vestibule's west front (w22999175 edge (460.9, -1340.1)-(461.2, -1360.2)), r 4, pose = the strip's
  VIEWS AltDigit1 (534, 125, yaw 28.61, pitch 10), view 'AltDigit1', reverse null, prompt copy }`. A door row has no `seg / inward / zone / arrive` and never enters the machine: `createGates` splits `mine` into gates (the machine), doors
  (`ctx.interact` items `gate-<id>`, [E] / USE, enabled while started and no travel or question is open; `onUse` asks the door question) and places; it returns null only when all three are empty. Accepting hands off to
  `index.html?cam=534,125,28.61,10` with `ho { via 'door', gate 'g3', car null, ret = where the player stood (the car's pose when he drove) }`; `planArrival` treats a door as a walk regardless of `ho.car` / `?fly` and resolves with
  `rect null` (direct at (534, 125)); `applyHandoffAtStart` therefore never calls `drive.arrive`: the player walks out of the metro at the rotunda on Vosstaniya Square; the "Back to North" link of `guard.back` returns to the vestibule.
  No reverse door: the strip's Chernyshevskaya is a closed generic terminus (`data/metro.js` STATIONS '1' chernyshevskaya `built: false`); the way back north is the border gate g1. Declining a door or a place never silences the border
  gate it travels through (`prompt(g, via, place)` books `markOpen / decline` only for via 'gate'); a failed preflight still cools the gate 30 s. The door pose is a 2-decimal literal because `core/handoff.js camParam` rounds `?cam=`
  to 2 decimals; `areas-test` compares it with the VIEWS row within 0.01 degrees and refuses a 3-decimal yaw.
* **CROSS_PLACES**: `src/world/area.js` lists 6 North places through g1 (Smolny Cathedral Digit7, Smolny Institute Digit8, the Tauride south gate / Kirochnaya Digit0, Chernyshevskaya Digit1, the Transfiguration Cathedral Digit2, the
  Suvorov Museum Digit9), `src/areas/north/area.js` 6 strip places through g2 (Vosstaniya Square Digit1, Palace Square Digit0, the Winter Palace Digit8, the Anichkov Bridge Digit5, Kazan Cathedral Digit9, the Library `Place.library`),
  each a copy of the other area's VIEWS row (2-decimal yaw). `main.js` appends one optgroup per other area to the `#places` list (strip: «Север · North», North: «Невский проспект · Nevsky strip», from the registry titles); a pick calls
  `ctx.gates.offerPlace`: the same confirm via 'place', the hand-off URL carries the place pose and the current mode's car (a flyer arrives standing), `planArrival` never clamps a place into the gate rectangle and resolves the pose as a
  WALKER's even with a car (the viewpoints are pedestrian spots: Palace Square and the Tauride gate have no drivable lane within 60 m along the heading), then `drive.arrive -> drive.place` parks the car at the nearest free spot within
  40 m exactly as the in-area list does with `goToView`; border crossings keep the rectangle and the lane rule. With `?gates=0` nothing is added (`ctx.gates` is null). Kirochnaya and the Tauride gate are the same VIEWS pose, so they are
  one row and the Smolny Institute fills the sixth; Digit5 (the garden) was deliberately not copied because the Tauride phases move it. The strip's world, golden, check counts, signatures and knock census are identical: the optgroup is a
  HUD addition. `window.__gates` gains `doors, places, offerPlace, isIndoors` for the browser tests.
* **Tests**: `gates-test` 29 -> 52, `handoff-test` 24 -> 25 (via door / place valid, a door carries no car), `areas-test` 72 -> 74 (the door row shape / play union, the pose pinned to VIEWS AltDigit1), `gate-hold-test` 22 identical, and the
  new `tools/cross-places-test.mjs` (section 5; 19 checks as the strip, 22 as the North, in CI and `npm run crossplaces`).

### 10.2 B, NorthFull polish: knock parity, the lit signs, the Kirochnaya stubs, doors and services

* **The cannon fence of the Transfiguration Cathedral** (`src/world/landmarksNorthFull/fence.js`) is `ctx.knock` instances through `lib/knock-inst.js`: 32 granite piers as anchored `bollard` records (mass 900, h 2.34, w 2.1, d 1.8,
  anchored 30, hitK 0.6, box collider 2.1 x 1.8 x 2.34; `onKnock` drops the pier's spans at 8 km/h, the `streets/barriers.js` pattern) and 24 chain spans of two strands as `chain` records with thin `fence` boxes (mass 12, h 0.8, w 7.2,
  d 0.12, anchored 0.5, hitK 0.05; collider 7.2 x 0.12 x 1.8 with `.fence = true`, which `people/nav.js` reads as a wall: the fence had NO colliders before M7). Two `knockInstances` calls, `lmNF-cannon-piers` (3 parts: granite box,
  bronze barrels, brass caps; 252 triangles per pier) and `lmNF-cannon-chains` (24 rods, 480 triangles per span); piers at i x 8.4 (i = -4 .. 4, no i = 0), spans from x + 0.65 for 7.1 m, none at i = -1 and i = 4 (the four approach
  paths stay open); same geometry and materials as the merged fence, the module's triangle total unchanged (583,153). Without `ctx.knock`: static InstancedMeshes, no registration. `check:north`: landmarksNorthFull meshes 110 -> 116,
  instanced meshes 0 -> 8, instances 120, colliders 366 -> 422; the page census 4,460 -> 4,516 (+32 bollards, +24 chains), then 4,513 after the props cascade of 10.3.
* **The vestibule signs** (ЧЕРНЫШЕВСКАЯ, МЕТРОПОЛИТЕН, М) use `litFascia`: the sign texture is the emissive map of a `lmD-sign-lit` toon material on the kit's `uNight` list (the strip's lit-sign pattern of landmarksA); `northfull-sign-probe`
  proves intensity 0 by day, 0.95 at night. The plates glow as backlit plates with dark letters (like the ticket-office and Galeria signs); lit letters on a dark plate would need a night variant of the fascia colours (a GPU look decision).
  The vestibule glazing is still unlit at night.
* **The Kirochnaya gate stubs** (`landmarksKirochnaya/landscape.js clipToKerb`): a campus lane's asphalt is clipped where it enters a non-campus carriageway (`L.roadsNear`, 1 m margin; the data `w/2` is the only kerb the module can read
  before `streets` builds). Measured with streets' `net.cover`: the stubs ended at Kirochnaya's centreline; the drawn asphalt reaches 4.31 / 3.82 m from it (parking pockets) against w/2 = 3.6; now the stub ends 0.3 (west) / 0.8 m (east)
  before the drawn kerb; `north-knock-test` check 5: 0 of 1,200 asphalt vertices on a data carriageway or on the streets asphalt (12 lie inside the stub's own junction-fillet mouth, counted apart). No D:/ path existed in the Kirochnaya
  files (the only one left under `src` is `landmarksTauride/data/ground.js:9`, the Tauride cluster's).
* **Door points and services**: `landmarksNorthFull/doors.js doorPoints(recs)` derives door points from the facade's OSM entrance nodes (81 lie on the claimed footprints) projected onto the outer edges and pushed 0.6 m out as
  `city/shops.js:143` does, else the parade-door rule of `city/walls.js` (round(len/20) per street-facing edge >= 5 m, no hash jitter; points closer than 1.5 m to an existing one are dropped). `landmarksNorthFull` publishes 86 door
  points for its 17 buildings (r946930 4, r5802451 3, the bookstore w96123542 8 parade points, the vestibule w22999175 3, one 0.6 m from the G3 door, 13 corridor houses 2-20 each), `landmarksOktyabrsky` 9 for the hall in the North only
  (7 mapped on w30324279 + 2 parade on the part w929214560; gated by the area's TABLE: `PRUNE_SERVICES.includes('landmarksOktyabrsky')` read through `lib/area-tables.js` like every other engine module (the strip's table does not list
  it, the North's does; the first version gated on `A.ID !== 'strip'` through a direct `import * as A from './area.js'`, replaced in fix round 1: no world module reads `area.js`); the strip's list stays empty and its check is
  identical). Smolny / Suvorov / Kirochnaya / NorthFull merge monuments / photo spots into `ctx.services.landmarks` like `landmarksD` (so `ctx.services.landmarks` is now created by `landmarksNorthFull`, the first landmark module: only
  the ORDER of the services list in `check:north` changes); `people-test --at smolny` with an eval script: 4 distinct pedestrians photographing the cathedral within 5 minutes (max 4 standing). Services census of the North

### 10.3 C, the missing landmarks: the Church of Simeon and Anna, the Kapodistrias monument

* **The church** (w96148838 + the chapel w96148878, (-304.1, -688.1), rot 0.105, 47 m bell tower; the chapel at (-332.9, -664.3)) is a new North-only module `src/world/landmarksBelinskyNorth.js` (+ `landmarksBelinskyNorth/groups.js`,
  claim `simeon-anna`): the source's `church()` and its four helpers verbatim (102_3d_piter `landmarksBelinsky.js` at 0568f7af; the circus, its sign and the forecourt paving are left out: the circus w28691685 at (-576, -695) is 150 m
  west of the North's extent), the church's ten materials, root `landmarks-belinsky-north`, service key `landmarksBelinskyNorth`, the church photo spot merged into `ctx.services.landmarks`. Why a separate module: n4spb's strip copy
  `landmarksBelinsky.js` builds the Ciniselli Circus only (its header leaves the church, 116 m past the play edge, to the generic city, and its `groups.js` has the circus row only), so the church group was MISSING from it, not merely
  outside the extent; a North module means zero edits to a file the strip executes. 27,955 triangles (BUDGET 34e3), 9 meshes, 2 colliders; it publishes no `entrances` (the source publishes none), so it is not in `PRUNE_SERVICES`.
  Listed between `landmarksTauride` and `streets` (it claims 2 buildings `city` must skip): `city` 787,005 -> 786,300 triangles (-705, colliders -39).
* **The Kapodistrias monument** n1372027612 with its apron is a fourth part of `landmarksOktyabrsky`: `monument.js, geo.js, trees.js, treegeo.js, leafmat.js, util.js` copied verbatim into the module folder, the KAP frame, `kapodistriasFig`,
  the source's painters / materials (`makeMonumentArt / addMonumentMats`, verbatim text moved into functions so that the strip's canvas pixels and materials stay identical) and the apron ring of `plaza.js` ported, all built ONLY where
  the area claims `kapodistrias`. The task's premise was wrong: n1372027612 IS in the strip's facade data (`data/city-east.js` MONUMENTS, kind memorial), so a gate on "the id is in the facade data" would have built the monument in the
  strip; the gate is the area's claim instead: `groups.js` exports `KAPODISTRIAS_GROUP` separately (`GROUPS_LIST` = the strip's claims, unchanged), the North's `area.js` claims `GROUPS_LIST + KAPODISTRIAS_GROUP`, and the module reads
  `L.GROUPS.landmarksOktyabrsky` (the `landmarksD` pattern of 8.3) to decide its parts, its materials and the apron. `check:north`: landmarksOktyabrsky 27,542 -> 47,001 triangles (monument 18,774 + the apron +149 in plaza; BUDGET 30e3
  -> 57e3), meshes 52 -> 90, materials 44 -> 61, canvas pixels +389,376, colliders 101 -> 114; the strip's row identical (`OKT_DEBUG` parts hall / plaza / october only). The monument's lantern lamp and litter bin are plain instances
  as in the source (not knockable). The monument faces SSW with 3 birches, 3 lilacs, 2 limes, a lantern, a bin and an information board in its setting (the source's facts, not re-researched).
* **The props cascade**: adding a module to the North list re-deals the single prop stream of `props/world.js` map-wide (1,560 -> 1,551 props in 29 types; benches 578 -> 576, the knock census -3): the source's behaviour, graded
  "(cascade)" by the snapshot oracle; the bake of M8 will show moved benches everywhere in the North.
* **Layout golden of the North** regenerated (GROUPS 7 -> 8, SUBGROUPS 14 -> 16, claims 532 -> 535, MODULE_KEYS + the module, the 25 probe columns gain the two new sub-group `at` points, identical on the first 501); the owns table
  gained the `landmarksBelinskyNorth` row (23 modules). Neither new claim lies in the 200 m band, so the band-landmark assertion of 8.4 is untouched. `LABELS`: `landmarksBelinskyNorth` «церковь Симеона и Анны», `landmarksOktyabrsky`
  «БКЗ «Октябрьский» и памятник Каподистрии».

## 11. M7 polish

The backlog of M7 after the preparation of section 10 (`m7-polish` = the three merges of the clusters A, B, C and this section). 11.1 to 11.3 are the blocks of the clusters, 11.4 the integration (what was merged, the strip proof, the oracles, the closed items of section 10), 11.5 the gaps left on purpose, 11.6 the items for the user.

### 11.1 Gates and texts (cluster A, branch `m7p-gates`)

* **A CROSS_PLACES row routed through a door gate** (`core/gates.js go()`): `crossPlaces()` keeps a row whose gate is a door (g3 leaves the North and leads to the strip), and `go()` took `dest = door ? g.pose : place`, so such a pick
  would have put the player at the metro rotunda, not at the place. The place now wins (`dest = via 'place' && place ? place : door ? g.pose : null`; a door E (`via 'door'`) is unchanged, a driver still walks: the door has no car).
  The question of a place asked through a door keeps the area's own title and scene (the door's say "metro" and "the rotunda"), names the place in the sights line and keeps the door's walk-out `fine` line.
  No real table names a door gate yet (both tables use g1 / g2): the pin is `tools/cross-places-test.mjs` section 5 (the real `core/gates.js` over fake DOM dependencies, a synthetic row `{...a North row, gate 'g3'}`: the question,
  the cam of the hand-off, `ho { via place, gate g3, car null }`, the return pose, a driver, the door E) and its mutant (a temporary copy of the source with the old line put back must fail the pin at the cam assertion), run under
  both areas (strip 22 / North 25 checks).
* **The boot guard per area** (`core/boot-guard.js`): the pre-start warning of a phone ("Heavy scene ...") had the strip's title on every page. `pageSizes(doc)` now also reads `data-card-title` of the heavy-scene note; the generator
  (`tools/gen-area-html.mjs`) writes it on a generated page as the intro kicker up to its first colon plus « · Before you start» (north.html: «Прогулка по северу города · Before you start»); `index.html` has no attribute and the
  strip falls back to `STRIP_CARD_TITLE` = «Прогулка по Невскому · Before you start», byte for byte what it was. The MB / GB of the card are those of the page's note as before (strip 51 / 1, North 36 / 1).
* **The way back on the arrival card**: `backLink(ho, { qName, base })` (core/gates.js, the code that was inline in `main.js`: the origin's page at `ho.ret` with `?cam=` and the quality tier, nothing new on the wire) feeds `guard.back`;
  `guard.arrivalLink()` (boot-guard.js, called by `main.js` when `ctx.handoff` exists) adds ONE link «Вернуться: <area> · Back to <area>» under the Continue row of the intro card (`#backlink`, created in script: no markup of
  index.html changes; a click is `location.replace`, one history entry like the hop itself). The fatal card keeps its button. The metro door leaves the player within 4 m of the vestibule door (`ho.ret` = where he stood), so the strip's
  arrival card returns him to the North at the door (a driver cannot use the door: the interact item is for walkers). Every hand-off arrival (g1 / g2 / a place / g3 / the dev g0) now shows the link, as the plan's arrival card
  wants; a page that is not reached by a hand-off shows none.
* **Registry sizes**: the strip row said `approxMB: 48` (the bake directory alone) and so did the g3 question's scene line ("около 48 МБ / about 48 MB"), against the page's announced total of ~51 MB; both say 51 now. The North row (36,
  generated into north.html) is untouched: the task text that started this run called the North row the one that says 48, the repository says the strip row does (section 10.7 listed it as a decision). `tools/areas-test.mjs` pins
  that every real area's registry `approxMB` equals the MB of its page's note (index.html 51, north.html 36), that every door gate's scene line names the registry MB of its destination in Russian and English, and the card titles
  above (fixture page, phone card on a fake DOM, `backLink`, `arrivalLink`).
* **`tools/areas-flow-test.mjs`**: the sections `g3fast` (the fast harness, no bake: the North page 3 m west of the door, the [E] chip, the question, the URL and `ho`, the strip arrival at AltDigit1 on foot, the arrival link, a click requests
  `north.html`) and `g3` (the same on the real pages with the real preflight, the link followed to the North page; needs `assets/baked/manifest.json` and `assets/baked/north/manifest.json`, otherwise a clear
  "needs a bake" skip that starts no browser). `--software` runs the browser on SwiftShader (CPU) instead of the GPU; the browser starts at the first page, so a skipping run starts none. `npm run areasflow:g3`.
  Run here with `--software` (no GPU): g3fast 10 checks green, g3 skipped (no bake in the checkout), arrival and required green (the fatal card still offers the way back); the walking sections (accept ...) cannot finish on SwiftShader
  (the walker does not reach the zone in 25 s: the same failure on the clean `feat/areas` tree), so they need the GPU.
* **Not changed on purpose**: `src/areas/north/page.json` (the card title is derived from its kicker; a dedicated field would be a data change), the strip's `index.html` (no markup), the `#places` list. The bake revision of the tree moves
  with this block (`main.js`, `boot-guard.js` and `north.html` are hashed; `gates.js` / `registry.js` are runtime-only): the bake is stale until M8 anyway.

### 11.2 B, landmarks polish (branch `m7p-landmarks`): Kirochnaya benches and stubs, the cross-module import, a stale comment

* **The Kirochnaya campus benches are knockable** (`landmarksKirochnaya/landscape.js`, closes the "5 campus benches" gap of 8.7 / 10): with `ctx.knock` the benches are `knockInstances` records of kind `bench` (iron ends + green slats as two instanced parts,
  one box collider 2.1 x 0.64 m, 1.1 m high, mass 50 kg, `minDv` 0.5; the pattern of `landmarksF/garden.js`); without `ctx.knock` the merged geometry is drawn as before (the bench drawing is one function now, the same boxes in the same order). The module's triangle
  service counts the instances (`K.tris` += template triangles x placements), so the North check differs in `landmarksKirochnaya` only in structure: meshes 176 -> 179, instanced meshes 0 -> 4, instances 0 -> 8, colliders 255 -> 259; the knock census of the North page
  4,513 -> 4,517 (benches 576 -> 580). **Four, not five**: the placement (1258, -1130) lies inside a mapped building (`L.insideBuilding`) and was skipped by the source's own filter, so it never drew a bench (the docs counted placements). Moving it is a look decision, not made here.

  vertices south of z -1236, the east one w515895296 6.25 m; a "missing west stub" does not reproduce on the CPU), but since T1 moved the streets (no parking pockets at the mouths any more) the 1 m margin left 0.94 m (west) / 1.03 m (east) of bare ground between the stub
  and the street. Now 0.249 m (west) / 0.343 m (east), no stub vertex inside a `pt-asphalt` triangle (0 of 60 each). The data's `w/2` is still the only kerb the module can read before `streets` builds, so the margin is an estimate: a later change of the streets' width
  at the mouths is caught by `north-knock-test` check 5b (0.05 .. 0.45 m, nothing inside). The GPU look (stub asphalt at y 0.051-0.055 against the street's 0.03, the 0.25-0.34 m slit) is still owed.

### 11.3 Tools and oracles (cluster C-tools-oracles, branch `m7p-tools`; `tools/` + CI only, no engine file)

Closes the tool gaps of 10.5-10.7. Nothing under `src/` changed: the strip's `npm run check` (25 modules), layout golden, streets / city signatures and environment regress are identical to the M6 baselines and to the
clean `feat/areas` (3bbdf8e), `check:north` (14 modules) identical to the clean tree in every column.

* **`claims-draw-audit` rule 1 grades the building itself.** The first version counted any centroid inside the footprint at any height, so a tree whose centroid fell in it, a floor or a neighbour's cornice satisfied
  "the owner draws". Now, for a claimed BUILDING of 5 m2 or more, an owner centroid counts only when it is (a) not foliage (the mesh-name chain matches `FOLIAGE_RE`: tree / trunk / hedge / lilac / reed ...),
  (b) higher than `--above` and deeper than `--inset` (the band of rules 2 and 3; both scale down for a small building: the inset to 0.3 x the deepest point of the footprint, the height to 0.4 x its mapped
  height, so a 5 m2 kiosk or a 1.2 m flight of steps is not graded by the band of a house), and (c) not deeper inside a building of another claim group (a neighbour's wall inside the shared outline, overlapping
  data outlines). A PART without a centroid of its own is covered by a drawn parent (`rec.part` / `parent`; the owner draws the wall on the shared outline): one `NOTE` line, ids with `--verbose`. Points, ways, lawns and
  tiny parts keep the loose count (warnings). A red message carries the breakdown of the centroids that do not count (`N foliage, N taken by a deeper neighbour, N within 1 m of the outline, N below the band`);
  `--verbose` rows show it per id (`[fol .. stolen .. shallow .. low ..]`, `LOOSE-ONLY` = loose centroids but none of its own). `--loose-rule1` grades the old way.
  Results: strip `RESULT: OK (10 warnings, 6 notes)` + 34 covered parts (was 43 warnings: 33 tiny parts moved from warning to the covered NOTE), North `OK (79 warnings, 3 notes)` (79 = garden points, benches and bins
  that the Tauride phases have not drawn yet). No claimed building of 5 m2 or more is drawn by foliage alone in either area today; the mutants prove that the rule would see it (all commands: `node tools/claims-draw-audit.mjs ...`,
  the North through `tools/area.mjs north`):

  | mutant | what it does | new rule | `--loose-rule1` (the old rule) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `--mutant undraw:r1453436` (strip, the General Staff: 11,265 centroids in the footprint, 185 its own, 11,026 on the outline) | the owner's OWN centroids of the building are left out of the audit's view | RED: `draws none of its own geometry ... (11080 centroids ... 11026 within 1 m of the outline, 54 below the band)` | **OK** (fooled) |
  | `--mutant undraw:w96364627` (North, a corridor house, the neighbours' walls on its outline) | same | RED (1838 centroids, 1833 on the outline) | **OK** |
  | `--mutant undraw:r3127704` (North, Kirochnaya: 1,942 centroids of which 1,491 are the campus trees) | same | RED (`1491 foliage`) | **OK** |

  | `--mutant unclaim:r5802451` (North) / `unclaim:r1453436` (strip) | the old rule 3 mutant | RED (13,605 triangles / 128) | - |

## 12. The world map and the area overlap rules (draft v1, 2026-10-24)

The start screen of the app becomes a MAP of Saint Petersburg (`maps/intro-draft.html`, built by `maps/intro-draft-src/`, README there). A visitor picks the part of the city to walk in, so that no scene has to hold the whole city (the performance problem of the source project). The areas of the map are the REAL municipal districts (okrugs, OSM `admin_level=8`, 111 of them), coloured by the 18 city districts; their borders follow streets and water. Targets: an area is about the size of area B (the North, 3.95 km2, +-20 % = 3.16 .. 4.74 km2); larger ones are split later (`split` in the data), smaller ones merged (`mergeGroup`).

### 12.1 Rules (decided by the project owner, implemented in `build-overlap.mjs`)

1. **Core.** An area is a municipal district; its size is compared with B by this core territory. The walkable territory of an area is the core PLUS the overlaps below (about +33 % on median); memory and bake budgets are counted on the walkable territory.
2. **Border streets and one block.** A street that is the border of two areas belongs to BOTH. Each area also reaches ONE block (the first row of blocks, at most 320 m deep, a block = a face of the street network) across the border street into its neighbour. So a visitor who walks along or across a border street is not prompted to enter the other area at once.
3. **Shared junctions.** Where THREE or more areas meet (a junction, a square, a station: found as border street cells with >= 3 areas within 160 m) the blocks around it belong to ALL those areas, including the diagonal block that rule 2 does not give. Example: the Moscow Railway Station / Ploshchad Vosstaniya (30.3603, 59.9311; 139 m from the station) belongs to Smolninskoye, Ligovka-Yamskaya, Vladimirsky and Liteyny. 97 such junctions in the draft (28 with 4 or more areas).
4. **Bridges.** A bridge whose two ends lie in different areas belongs to both (the bridge cells and their approaches within 160 m of the ends).
5. **Water is reachable** (water transport comes later). Every water cell belongs to the areas whose shore is nearest, up to 2 km from the shore; an area whose shore is within 320 m of the nearest one also owns the cell (a river or canal between two areas belongs to both). Open water farther than 2 km from any shore is not owned: it will become separate water zones.
6. **The hover rule on the map:** the red line shows the whole walkable territory of the hovered area (core + overlaps + bridges); the dashed red line shows its water.

### 12.2 What follows for the scenes

* The gates between scenes (section 2) move from the border line to the OUTER edge of the overlap: the prompt appears only when the visitor leaves the walkable territory of the current area, not when he crosses a district border. The 200 m shared band between A and B (section 4) is the first instance of rule 2.
* A building, bench, car or person that stands in an overlap exists in both scenes (the same claims, the same data: the cut of the area data takes the walkable territory, not the core).
* Sizes: the classes `b` / `large` / `big` / `small` of the map are computed on the core; the walkable territory is shown next to it in the area card.
* Data of the first scenes comes from the source project (`D:/git2/agi-fun-tests/102_3d_piter`): the map marks which areas it already covers (19 of 111 fully, the whole historic core) and its authored sights.

### 12.3 Known weaknesses of the draft

* Blocks outside the source's raw OSM box are built from the main streets only (no residential streets), so "one block" there is capped by the 320 m rule rather than by a real block.
* Unnamed or missing border streets in the data: a few border segments have no street name in the border list.
* The municipal borders are the official ones; some are odd and will be re-cut together with the split / merge of areas.

## 13. The first two areas of the world map: Palace-Liteyny and Smolninskoye (v1.0.0, 2026-10-25)

The strip and the North were re-cut to two areas of the map of section 12. Ids `palace` (Дворцовый–Литейный, map area 98, page `index.html`, the start page of the app) and `smolny` (Смольнинское, map area 96, page `smolny.html`).
The old two scenes are PARKED (registry `parked: true`): `strip` (`strip.html`, now also the TEMPLATE of every generated page, `tools/gen-area-html.mjs`), `north` (`north.html`), `src/world/area.js`, `src/world/data`, `src/areas/north`, `assets/baked/`, `assets/baked/north/` stay as they were
(nothing deleted) for the neighbour areas that are cut later. No gate leads to a parked area, `npm run bake` (`--area all`) skips them, and their bakes are stale against the new revision until they are baked by name (`--area strip` / `north`).

### 13.1 Shape
The play rectangles of an area are its WALKABLE TERRITORY on the map (core + overlaps, section 12.1): `node tools/area-shape.mjs --k 98 --js palace` rasterises the outline of the map (`maps/intro-draft.html`, embedded data) in 80 m cells (a cell is in when 4 of 9 sample points are inside:
a slight outward bias, so a border street is never cut off) and merges the cells into rectangles. Since the owner's water rule (2026-10-25: all water is reachable) the territory is the land outline PLUS the map's water zones (`wz`: open water within 2 km of the shore, section 12.1 rule 5; `tools/area-shape.mjs` unions both, `--no-water` gives the land alone): 40 rectangles / 7.0 km2 for Palace-Liteyny and 41 / 9.4 km2 for Smolninskoye (land alone: 5.3 and 7.0 km2); the raster is tidied before merging (an empty cell with 3 of 4 edge neighbours in is filled, a cell without an edge neighbour is dropped: no invisible one-cell walls, no corner-only islands). Both rows also carry the rectangle `ST` (x 400..835, z 160..1060): the Moscow station complex belongs to both areas whole,
because the rail module needs complete platforms (the station is a shared junction of both). The rows are pasted into `src/areas/registry.js` (`play`); the data of the area is cut from the source project in one go: `node tools/area-data-cut.mjs --area palace` (and `smolny`), then `tools/strip-data-known.mjs`
for `known-data.js` (commands in section 6 of docs/AREAS-DATA.md). A part file per rectangle (`city-p01.js` ... `city-s61.js`).

### 13.2 Modules
`src/areas/palace/area.js` starts from the strip's area module: the landmark modules whose sights lie in the territory (A, B, C, E, F, G, I, K, Castle, Belinsky, bridges, the library of D, and from the North: NorthFull for Dom Muruzi, the Transfiguration Cathedral and Chernyshevskaya, BelinskyNorth) with their GROUPS;
St Isaac's, the Admiralty, the Senate (H, J), the metro network and Oktyabrsky are left out. `src/areas/smolny/area.js` starts from the North's module (Smolny, Suvorov, Kirochnaya, Tauride with its hooks from `../north/hooks.js`, Oktyabrsky) and adds landmarksA, landmarksB (only the sub-groups whose buildings
are in its data), `rail` and the station zones. A module that is group-aware (`L.groupMembers(key)`, `if (!recs.length) return`) builds only what the area claims. `node tools/area.mjs <id> tools/check.mjs --area-modules` builds all modules headlessly.

**Known difference of the claims in an overlap (review finding 12).** Section 12.2 says a building in an overlap exists in both scenes with "the same claims". That holds for the Moscow station complex (both areas claim `landmarksA`), but not for the sub-groups that only Palace-Liteyny lists: `landmarksB` `nevsky-corner` (Nevsky 100, 100 lit I, 104, 108) and the `north-corridor-houses` of `landmarksNorthFull` (Vosstaniya 17 / 19 / 40 / 44, Radishcheva 40 and 5-7, Mitavsky 10, Baskov 41 / 29 ...) have members that lie inside the play rectangles of Smolninskoye. There they are GENERIC `city` buildings (not authored, no claim): `smolny/area.js` claims only the sub-groups that belong to its sights (`nevsky-centre`, `north-chernyshevskaya`) and deliberately NOT partial copies of the palace sub-groups (a partial group would split one landmark between two modules). The Nevsky Atrium (r15081493) is the opposite case: it lies wholly outside Smolninskoye (x 159..222, z 86..157, west of S27), so only Palace-Liteyny claims it; `landmarksB/atrium.js` returns without a claim.

### 13.3 Objects
Buildings, benches, bins, kiosks, cars, people are movable and breakable exactly as before (the same modules and registrations); an object in an overlap exists in both areas, a hand-off does not carry the state of objects.

### 13.4 Cars
The four cars (coupe, pickup, Zhiguli, roadster) stand ONLY at the central station, the Moscow station (`GARAGE.poses`, the strip's poses). An area whose territory does not contain the station holds ONE car, the purple Zhiguli (`lada`: VAZ-2106 in eggplant), at its spawn. Both first areas contain the station, so both have the four.

### 13.5 Place teleports off, respawn stays
The Places list and the view keys (1..0, Shift, Alt) are off (`?teleports=1` restores them for development and the tools): the player walks to the places himself. **R (respawn at the start) and the touch menu's Respawn stay**: the way back from anywhere. The page help lines carry movement keys and R only.

### 13.6 Version
`src/core/version.js` (1.0.0 and up, SemVer), shown top right on the preloader; `node tools/version.mjs patch|minor|major "note"` bumps it, `package.json` and `CHANGELOG.md`; the rules of the levels are in `CLAUDE.md`.

### 13.7 Water is reachable (the owner's world rule, 2026-10-25)

**The rule.** Every river, canal, pond and fountain basin is walkable and drivable, in every area, present and future. Water is never a wall: not for the walker, not for the car, not for the camera, not for the territory or a check. Only fences, parapets, railings and low walls block, and every one of them is
BREAKABLE through the knock system (a car breaks a bay off at the foot, it topples, the collider is replaced by a rest box of at most 0.42 m: the gap is passable). CLAUDE.md carries the rule for the future sessions. Water transport comes later and will use these waters.

**What the player meets.** A walker steps off an open quay edge (no fence drawn = no collider) or through a broken bay, falls into the trench and walks on the bed: from 0.45 m of water he wades (slower, a weaker jump of 3.0 m/s), at 1.7 m the camera is still at eye height above the bed; the cheap underwater tint is the
fixed overlay of `core/water-view.js` (one div, opacity eased from `physics.waterLevel` at the camera). A car drives off a ramp or through a broken railing, falls (the existing fall damage events of the damage model), lands on the bed, drives there with a water drag, and cannot climb a trench wall back out (a rise limit; R respawns).
Ponds and fountain basins are the same with shallow beds. The flight landing is never refused. The play-edge slide (`clampToPlay`) is unchanged.

**The engine (shared files).**
* `core/physics.js`: `new Physics(heightAt, waterway)` (the second argument is `L.waterLevelAt`, wired in `core/ctx.js`: the waterways of `WATERWAYS`, level 0, beds fontanka 1.4, griboedov 1.4, moika 1.5, winter 1.2, swan 1.2, moat 1.2, neva 2.6 deep) and `addWater({ ring, holes, level } | { fn })` for a landmark's own water (ponds, basins);
  `waterLevel(x, z)` answers the surface level or null. This is THE hook for a later water transport: a boat reads the same answers and floats at the level instead of sinking.
* `core/player.js`: `depth`, `under`, `wade` (WADE_MIN 0.45, WADE_DEEP 1.7), the jump in water; `landingBlocked` no longer refuses water (the `noLanding` field and `HOOKS.noLanding` are removed: HOOKS has fifteen keys; `main.js`, `lib/area-tables.js`, `areas/north/hooks.js`, `hooks/water.js` follow).
  `core/handoff.js`: the edge-gate probe no longer counts the river as blocked.
* `world/drive/handling.js`: water as terrain (`cliff()`, `settle()`, `FALL_G`, `DROP_MAX`, `RISE_MAX`, `WATER_K`, `WATER_DEEP`). **The car's floors over the water** (the reconciliation with the Troitsky leaf, 13.8): `groundAt(x, z, ref, deckRef)` takes (1) a deck of a deck service when the car is on or above it (`deckAt`: `ref > deck - 1.2`; under a bridge the car is on the bed), (2) a kerb / pavement slab up to 0.3 m, (3) a walkable floor BELOW the road over a trench (a pier deck, the lower landing of a quay descent: the floor the walker stands on, found from above only: `surfaceAt` is asked from `min(ref, 0.5)`, so a car on the bed under it does not read it), (4) the bed, (5) the asphalt. `car.reset` (every placement: drive.place / arrive / respawn, the hop arrival) asks with `deckRef = 1e9`: a point of the plan is placed ON the deck above it whatever its height (the old test `car.y > deck - 1.2` read the river bed under every deck above 1.17 m, because reset starts from y 0.03). A drop of more than `DROP_MAX` is a fall (damage from 5 m/s), except that the tilt clamp of the axles (`|yF - g0| > DROP_MAX` holds the car level on its middle at an edge) is off where a deck lies under both the axle and the middle: a leaf raised by 25 degrees steps 0.6 m over the wheelbase and the body must pitch with it. A car that reaches the free tip of a leaf that is not yet down (it hangs up to 0.5 m over the pier) falls the 0.5 m, a car that drives off the far pier into the open span falls into the trench (`troitsky-test` (c); the river is no wall, so the old "the car stops at the tip" no longer holds and the closing-leaf test (b) lets the driver wait and creep). Tests: `water-reachable-test` section 8 (placements on 200 deck points, pier decks and landings placed / dropped on, the walker on the same floors).
* `core/knock.js`: `ghost` records (a collider with no drawing, breakable like a bay); `world/lib/knock-inst.js`: `part.color(spot, i, k)`.
