* NEW `world/lib/fence-bays.js`: `knockBays(ctx, { name, bays, parts, collider, rec })`: a fence is a row of straight bays, each with its thin collider (flagged `fence`: the pedestrian nav reads it as a wall), its instances in the per-cell dynamic meshes and ONE anchored knock record.
  The design rule: **a collider exists if and only if a fence is drawn** where it stands. Where nothing is drawn the edge is open; an old invisible wall became a breakable ghost bay (`bay.ghost`), never a solid.
* `areas/north/hooks/keep-out.js`: `carKeepOut` is the garden minus its ponds (a pond cell is open).

**Knock coverage (the audit, every fence / parapet / railing / low wall within 12 m of water).** Breakable bays or ghost bays: `environment/quay.js` (railing posts, panels and tubes of every bank style, the Neva granite parapet in 2.2 m sections, slope rails, descent cheeks, pier railings), `environment/boats.js` (a pier rail only where the pier is not attached),
`bridges/{rails,arch,foot,palace,deck}.js` (rails, parapets, faces, closed banks; the one batch of `rails.js` drops a ghost bay that a drawn bay already covers), `landmarksC/{bridge,closure}.js` (Anichkov: panel bays, abutment ghost stretches, the four tumbas as records, the quay gaps),
`landmarksGarden/{fence,ground}.js` (Neva columns and spans, Moika bays, the shut gate bay, basin rims, pond lattice rails), `landmarksK/{fence,grove,garden}.js` (Winkler and spear bays, piers, the pond mesh fence, the Rossi bridge railing), `landmarksCastle/bridge.js` (moat rails), `landmarksMars/palace.js` (forecourt fence),
`landmarksF/garden.js` (the Kazan fountain rim and its pillar), `landmarksTauride/{fence,bridges,monuments}.js` (the tall panels, the bridge rails, the fountain rim), `northBridges.js`, `eastBridges.js`, `landmarksTroitsky/{bridge,leaf}.js` (13.8: panels, parapets, kerb bays, candelabra, poles, and the bays of the moving leaf), `streets/quay.js` (guard stones and the Neva bollards with their chain). The Tauride ponds have no shore colliders at all (`ponds.js`: built only with `opts.colliders`).
Solid on purpose (structures or vegetation, listed with reasons in the ALLOW list of the test): lawn hedges, the clipped lattice hedges of the Summer Garden, the Krylov railing and its shut leaf, the pier-head bastions and the barrier kerbs of the Palace Bridge, the café terrace fences (props), the Kazan bollard rings, the Tauride low and pitch fences and piers,
the gate leaves and the eagle columns of the Summer Garden Neva fence, the two obelisks of the Troitsky Bridge (granite monument blocks), the castle moat bridge decks (low stone decks on the bed, a pier for a wader). Open: a bridge's posts, handrails and pylons stay DRAWN after their panel broke (merged in the static bridge mesh; only the collider is gone).

**What NPCs still do.** Pedestrians, traffic and animals are unchanged: `nav.blocked` reads `env.waterAt` (water stays an obstacle for the routes), the pedestrian nav keeps its baked snapshot of the colliders (a knocked fence stays an obstacle for the NPCs until the next bake), traffic stays on its roads, the ducks on their lanes. No nav re-bake was done.

**Known gap.** The slope banks (the Swan Canal and the like) are strips of grass slope, not terrain the walker follows: he stays at y 0 over them.

**Test.** `npm run water` = `node tools/area.mjs <palace|smolny> tools/water-reachable-test.mjs [--quick]` (CPU, real colliders and physics, `tools/lib/headless-world.mjs` builds the world without the city, traffic, people, wildlife): water cells are inside the territory, not blocked, finite ground, not keep-out, and no wall of colliders cuts a body of water;
a walker enters an open edge and wades, cannot climb back; a knocked bay opens the way; the car breaks through at 40 km/h, falls, lands on the bed with the drag and cannot climb out; a flight landing is allowed; the AUDIT of thin tall colliders near water; in the Smolninskoye the pond registry and free bank corridors that end wet.
Changed to the rule: `views-check`, `area-garage-check`, `handoff-test`, `areas-flow-test`, `north-hooks-test`, `north-keepout-test`, `north-knock-test`, `tauride-height-test` (+ `tauride/lib/height-harness`, `tauride/{pond,bridge,furniture}-test`). NPC tests keep avoiding water.

### 13.9 What `tools/areas-test.mjs` pins about the live areas (tools-only; `tools/lib/edge-geometry.mjs`)
The template of every generated page is `strip.html` (the default page of the browser tools too: `resolveArea({})` is the strip; `index.html` is the generated page of `palace`, `--area palace`); `page.json`, the generated page on disk and the throw-away-root CLI run (it generates every real area) are checked for `palace` and `smolny` like for the north, and their help lines carry movement keys and R but no digit key (13.5). Both area modules export `TEST` (claimed ids per landmark module, the exact `VIEWS` keys, the HUD names, a road probe) for `layout-test` / `landmarks-groups-check`. The registry pins: each territory is ONE edge-connected piece with no enclosed hole and no corner-only island (the invisible wall across Pushkinskaya and the four islands of the first cut cannot return); both areas carry the same station rectangle `ST`; an edge gate runs each way for every pair of live areas (`e1`, `e2`: no `seg` / `inward` / `arrive`, a reverse that leads back and names it, the territories overlap) and no gate leads from a live area to a parked one. The LENS of an edge gate samples the boundary of FROM every 20 m (8 probe directions at 40 m, the 60 m `edgeIn` rule of 14.2) and classifies it as hops / dead (ground of TO behind a wall that does not hop) / world edge (nothing to hop to): at 320efb6 palace to smolny hops over 4,040 m of 17,000 m (dead 440 m, the worst strip 220 m), smolny to palace over 4,000 m of 18,120 m (dead 880 m, worst 260 m, all of it beside the thin north rim and the station notch); the test requires at least 1,000 m hopping, no dead strip over 300 m and at most 10 percent of the hopping boundary within 100 m of the landing where the reverse gate would fire (the 80 m overlap at x 400 .. 480, z -2000 .. -1840 is the weak spot, 160 m). The arrival of a hop (same x, z, yaw, in a child per destination) is resolved by `handoff.resolveArrival` with the destination layout at every hopping point (walk, fly, and a car along its lane in both senses): on dry ground all must be valid; a hop into WATER must not fall back to the HERO (world rules: the check does not treat a river as a failure, `src/core/handoff.js` `makeProbe` still blocks it, so those two checks stay red until the probe is fixed). `tools/claims-draw-audit.mjs` and `tools/check.mjs` carry tables for `palace` and `smolny` (known notes with their reason, the triangle tripwires at the measured counts + about 15 %).

### 13.8 The Troitsky Bridge (module `landmarksTroitsky`: a bridge that OPENS, and that the player can use while it opens)

The Troitsky Bridge (Троицкий мост, 554 m bank to bank on the OSM axis, 23.6 m wide) lies wholly inside the territory of `palace` (its deck u -1.6 .. 555 is inside the play rectangles; **no registry row was changed**; the territory ends 4 m beyond the north abutment). The module draws all of it and opens its one bascule leaf on a schedule of the game clock.
`'landmarksTroitsky'` is in the palace `MODULES` after `bridges` (the stop lines need `streets`, its `deckY` wraps `bridges.deckY`) and before `traffic` / `people` (they read it); `LABELS` has its loading text; `GROUPS.landmarksTroitsky` = `landmarksTroitsky/groups.js` (OWNS_GROUPS = true: the seven OSM ways `w142545019 / 020`, `w309915522`, `w142545023 / 024`, `w202626988 / 991`, so `streets` leaves the deck to the module; the lanes stay in the lane graph); `src/world/lib/owns-table.js` is regenerated (`node tools/owns-table.mjs`);
`WATER.bridgeZones` has the row `troitsky` (own, key neva, axis 156.99, len 554: the quay is open at both banks, no invisible fallback deck); `HOOKS.deckServices` lists `landmarksTroitsky` (`areas/north/hooks.js makeDeckServices`: cars and pedestrians ask it first).

**One leaf, not two (a deviation from the first brief, on the research).** The source project's dossier (`docs/research/petro-bridges.md`, photos) says: ten spans from the Mars Field bank, the **single-leaf** bascule is the second (43.2 m, `u 15.2 .. 58.2`), hinged on the narrow first pier (the counterweight on the bank side swings down into the pit under the first span), the free end resting on the 12.1 m granite pier. The model follows it: the leaf rises toward the river, the gap it opens is 28 m wide at 70 degrees.

| File | Content |
|---|---|
| `landmarksTroitsky.js` | `build(ctx)`: the static bridge, the leaf, the barriers, the service, the frame loop (clock step, leaf transform and floor, barriers, toast, the people hooks, the carry / slip of the player or his car) |
| `landmarksTroitsky/frame.js` | PURE: the frame (origin on the Mars Field bank line, +u toward the Petrograd side, bearing 336.99), the cross-section, the closed deck profile `yDeck(u)`, the leaf kinematics (`leafY(u, theta)`, `leafFoot`, `leafSlope`, `carryDu`, `leafVy`), the footing rule |
| `landmarksTroitsky/schedule.js` | PURE: the clock (`createClock`, the constants `CFG`, `ease`) |
| `landmarksTroitsky/service.js` | PURE: the published service, the floor ramps, the dynamic boxes, the stop lines, `stepWalker` / `stepCar` (carry and slip) |
| `landmarksTroitsky/bridge.js` | the static bridge (a port of the source's `bridges/troitsky.js` with the bascule span cut out): piers, five steel spans, the granite viaduct, abutments, two obelisks (the only solid structure), the floor ramps, and EVERYTHING THAT BREAKS (see "Fences" below): whip panels and granite parapet sections (the rail batch), the barrier kerb bays, 16 candelabra, 20 contact poles and 22 one-globe lamps as knockable instances (`lib/knock-inst.js`, `lib/fence-bays.js`) |
| `landmarksTroitsky/leaf.js` | the moving group (deck, the deck strip under the kerb, pavements, tram rails, the deep girder, soffit, the posts and top rail of the railing, one lamp pair, one pole, the counterweight), its three floor ramps, and the 56 BAYS of its two rails and two kerbs (knock records that ride on it, with dynamic boxes: see "Fences") |
| `landmarksTroitsky/gates.js` | the barrier arms (8 booms: 2 gates x 2 carriageway + 2 pavement) with red lamps; solid, breakable knock records since 13.11 (`lib/bascule-gates.js`) |
| `landmarksTroitsky/groups.js` | the claim |

**The schedule (`schedule.js CFG`, every number tunable at the top of the file; `?troitsky=open|closed|auto`, default auto, also `service.setMode(m)`).** AUTO: the bridge opens when it has stood closed for `DAY_GAP` = 300 s of game time in the daylight / overcast presets, for `NIGHT_GAP` = 90 s in the white-night preset (`ctx.shared.uNight >= NIGHT_ON` = 0.5): the real navigation window shortened for play, plus a short daytime demonstration every five minutes; the first opening comes `FIRST_AT` = 100 s after the page started. One cycle: **WARN** (>= 14 s: the barrier arms drop in 5 s, the STOP flag is up, the leaf has not moved; the leaf waits, at most `WARN_MAX` = 75 s in all, until the span is clear of NPC vehicles and pedestrians) -> **OPENING** (`OPEN_T` = 80 s, `theta = 70 degrees * smootherstep(p)`, zero speed at both ends, at most 1.64 degrees/s) -> **HOLD** (25 s; mode `open` stays open) -> **CLOSING** (80 s, the mirror; `setMode('closed')` while opening reverses `p` without a jump) -> **CLEAR** (6 s with the leaf down, then the arms lift and the flag drops). A full cycle is about 205 s. The HUD toast "Троицкий мост разводят · The Troitsky Bridge is opening" appears once per cycle, to a player within 700 m of the bridge. No sound, no flags.

**Published service** `ctx.services.landmarksTroitsky`: `state()` -> `{ phase: 'closed' | 'opening' | 'open' | 'closing', stage ('idle' | 'warn' | 'opening' | 'hold' | 'closing' | 'clear'), angle (rad), t (0..1), stop, barrier (0 up .. 1 down), mode, cycles }`; `deckY(x, z)` (the asphalt level: the profile on the closed deck, the LIVE surface of the leaf where it stands, `null` in the gap an open leaf leaves and outside the deck: whatever lies there is the engine's business, a fall goes to `heightAt`); `deckLift(x, z)` (a walker's feet above it: 0.2 on the granite pavement, 0.03 on the asphalt); `setMode`, `mode()`, `stop`, `theta`, `gates`, `stepBlocked(x0, z0, x1, z1)`, `inSpan`, `inGated`, `onLeaf`, `clock`, `leaf`, `stats` (`tris`, `stopLines`, `swept` ...). Also: `services.bridges.deckY` is wrapped (traffic, parked cars) and `services.bridges.list / masts / spans` get the Troitsky entries (the traffic prefilter, the pedestrians' long-bridge logic).

**The NPC world (no engine rewrite; what was touched is listed below).**
* *Cars and trams:* `addStopLines` appends one stop line per carriageway lane toward the span (gate S at u = 6 on the first span for northbound, gate N at u = 76 beyond the landing pier for southbound) to `streets.stopLines` and wraps `streets.signalState / signalInfo`: the signal `troitsky-s / -n` reads `red` while the STOP flag is up (the sim and the tram sim already stop at a red stop line; those already past the line drive off). The queue stands before the line.
* *Pedestrians:* `people/nav.js` gets the long-bridge pavement band `LBAND.troitsky = [9.85, 11.8]` (one table entry; the module publishes the span), so people walk the deck (about 100 on it at the start); the service then wraps four functions of `people.sim.nav` from its frame loop: `fenceNear` / `segBlocked` (a step INTO the closed span through a gate line is a wall, a step out is free), `densityFactor` (the part between the gates is a thinned zone while the flag is up: nobody is placed there and an agent inside picks goals outside, so the span drains by itself), `reliefY` (the people on a moving leaf ride its surface); an NPC still on the leaf when it is steeper than the walkers' slip angle is set down outside the south gate (every 0.5 s).
* The arms are SOLID and BREAKABLE since 13.11 (they were a picture): knock records with a dynamic box while they are down (`lib/bascule-gates.js`); a car below 15 km/h is stopped, above it breaks the boom, which stays broken until the next cycle starts. A car can drive up the rising leaf as a RAMP (13.11); the slip limit of a car is 45 degrees now (25 here before the stunts).

**The leaf as a surface.** Physics floor: three `walk ramp` items (the roadway + barrier-kerb strip, two pavements at +0.2) whose `cx, cz, hd, yA, yB` are overwritten each frame by `placeRamps` (an item mutated at run time like a conveyor's `dir`; the footprint of the raised leaf lies inside the closed one, so the grid cells stay valid); `surfaceAt` / `core/player.js` / the car's `groundAt` (`deckY` under both axles: the body pitches with the slope, `drive/handling.js` unchanged) need no change. Colliders: the 56 bays of the two rails and the two barrier kerbs are DYNAMIC boxes (`ctx.physics.addDynamic` -> `refreshDynamic`, produced only while the player is within 160 m), one per bay, each the `src` of the bay's own knock record: see "Fences" below. **Carry:** each frame the module sets a player (or his car) that stands on the leaf where the rotating leaf carries him (`carryDu`: a material point moves toward the hinge as the leaf rises) and on its surface: continuous contact, the vertical speed handed over is `(u - 15.2) * dTheta/dt` <= 1.23 m/s. **Slip rule:** beyond a slope of **35 degrees (walker)** / **25 degrees (car)** a body slides down toward the hinge: `a = g (sin phi - tan(limit) cos phi)` (zero at the limit, continuous), along-slope speed capped at 7 m/s (walker) / 16 m/s (car); a walker's own uphill step of the frame is taken back, a car's velocity along u is held at or below the slide (the throttle cannot climb a steep leaf); the slide ends on the first pier (flat). They begin at theta = 33.6 degrees (walker) and 23.6 degrees (car): a car on the leaf is thrown off by the opening long before a walker.

**Shared engine files changed** (all small): `src/areas/north/hooks.js` (one name in `makeDeckServices`), `src/world/people/nav.js` (one `LBAND` entry), `src/areas/palace/area.js` (MODULES, LABELS, GROUPS, one `bridgeZones` row), `src/world/lib/owns-table.js` (generated), `tools/check.mjs` (a `BUDGET` line: 120 k), `package.json` (the script). `drive/handling.js`, `core/physics.js`, `core/player.js`, `traffic.js`, `people/sim.js` are untouched.

**Fences (the world rule of 13.7: every fence, parapet, railing and low wall of the bridge breaks; audited by `tools/water-reachable-test.mjs` and `tools/troitsky-test.mjs` sections 7, 8).**
* *The fixed bridge.* The `whip` panels of the steel part (cut to the candelabra pedestals, so no invisible wall stands in a gap), the granite parapet sections of the south span and the viaduct (2.4 m, `rails.parapet`: a swept box) and the parapets of the two wings at the south landing (`closeBank(..., rails)`) are bays of the rail batch (`bridges/rails.js` -> `lib/fence-bays.js`): each has its thin collider and ONE knock record (a panel: 60 kg, breaks at 12 km/h; a parapet section: 700 kg, 18 km/h). No static box stands under them any more (the old 8 m boxes under the whole railing line were the 178 non-breakable walls the audit found; the quay beyond the wings is open, as at the Palace Bridge).
* *The barrier kerb* (0.5 m granite between the roadway and a pavement, steel on the leaf): bays of about 4 m (`unitBox` instances, 900 kg, break at 30 km/h, `fence: false` for the pedestrian nav as before); the collider starts 3 m from the bank lines as before, the deck strip under the kerb stays when a bay is gone; the pavement-high step at the two bank ends is merged and has no collider.
* *Furniture.* The 16 candelabra (a pedestal and its three-globe candelabrum are ONE rigid knockable item: 2.4 t, break at 45 km/h, the collider is the footprint of the pedestal) and the 20 contact-wire poles of the median (520 kg, 30 km/h; the pole on the leaf rides with it) are knockable instances like the 22 one-globe lamps. The barrier arms have no collider (the player may drive through them): nothing to break.
* *Solid by design:* the two obelisks of the south entrance (granite monument blocks 2 x 2 m, 8 m tall, on the embankment: structure; `TROITSKY_STRUCTURE` and the ALLOW list of the water test say why).
* *THE LEAF.* Its 34 rail panels (2.5 m) and 22 kerb pieces (about 4 m) are knock records like the fixed ones, with their instances in WORLD-frame knock meshes that `leaf.set(theta)` re-poses whenever the angle changes: for every STANDING bay the record origin, the rest offsets of its parts (`off` / `q` / `s`, core/knock.js `makePart`) and the instance matrix are written from the leaf transform, and its collider is a dynamic box (`service.js leafBayAt`, a box that follows the slope, produced within 160 m of the player) which is the record's own `src` (the protocol of drive/collision.js: a car hit goes through the ordinary `invMassRatio` / `applyCarImpulse`, nothing leaf-specific). **A hit at ANY angle breaks the bay from the pose it has now** (it topples about its foot, sparks and clank; a bay that falls over the open river sinks and is gone). **The rule for what is left lying:** the debris of a broken bay lies where it fell (a rest box <= 0.42 m, a car drives over it) only while the leaf lies closed; as soon as the leaf moves (theta > 0.001) it is swept away (rest box removed, instance hidden: the bridge crew); a broken bay is never rebuilt (nothing in the world is repaired); the posts and the top rail strip of the railing stay drawn, as on every bridge. The leaf deck itself is untouched: the three floor ramps, the carry and the slip rule work as before, and the car / walker tests of 13.8 pass unchanged. Without `ctx.knock` (a harness) the leaf keeps its old plain path (panels in the moving mesh, plain dynamic boxes): never the game.

**Weight:** about 109,700 triangles in `check` (the static bridge, the leaf 4.7 k, the knockable instances counted per instance), 983 colliders, 94 meshes of which 29 instanced (73 / 7 before: the candelabra, the poles, the kerb, the parapets and the leaf bays are knock meshes, one draw per type and 384 m cell: 21 draws more on a 554 m bridge); 776 knock records (22 lamps, 16 candelabra, 20 poles, 254 kerb bays, 320 + 88 panel and parapet bays, 56 leaf bays ...); the leaf and the barriers are meshes moved by transform (no geometry rebuild), the 56 bays are re-posed by matrix writes only while the angle changes.

**Tests (CPU only).** `npm run troitsky` (`node tools/area.mjs palace tools/troitsky-test.mjs`, about 12,400 checks): the wiring and the claim; the kinematics (the closed leaf is the deck profile, the height rises with the angle, the slope is the elevation, the carry, the vertical speed of a carried body <= 1.3 m/s over the cycle); the schedule (the flag is up >= 14 s before the leaf moves and drops after it lies and the arms are up, theta is continuous, the easing, the modes, the night rule, the wait for a clear span); the service (`deckY` = the profile on the closed deck, continuous in space and time, the live leaf at several angles, `null` in the gap); the stop lines and the pedestrian gate test; the REAL module built headless (`tools/tauride/lib/stub-env.mjs`): triangles, the physics floor equals `deckY` at every angle, the dynamic rails follow the leaf, the real `Player` and the real `drive/handling.js` car on the leaf through a cycle (contact, carry, slip at 35 / 25 degrees, the car drives onto the closing leaf). `node tools/area.mjs palace tools/check.mjs landmarksTroitsky` builds it (and `--area-modules` the whole page). Section 7 of the test: no solid but the two obelisks is left (every box and post of the module is a knock record), the bays of the leaf sit on it at every angle (instance, record origin, rest offsets and dynamic box), a car at 50 km/h breaks the barrier kerb and a railing panel of the CLOSED deck (u 100 east and west, the crown) and drives off the bridge into the Neva, a car at 8 km/h is held by the kerb; section 8: the rule of the leaf (a bay hit toward the roadway lies on the pavement, toward the water sinks, the debris is swept when the leaf moves, a standing bay hit at 23 degrees breaks from its raised pose, the broken bays stay broken when the leaf lies again, a walker leaves the leaf through the gap and an intact panel holds him, a car breaks a panel of the closed leaf and falls into the Neva). `npm run water` audits the module whole (`6 AUDIT (the Troitsky Bridge)`).

**Not done / follow-ups:** sound (the bell, the leaf's drive), boats passing through the open span, navigation lights and the night illumination of the raised leaf, a visible counterweight pit (the weight swings down behind the first span's granite face), the walkers' horizontal carry for NPC people (they ride the surface but are not carried), trams that run over the bridge are stopped by the stop lines only when a tram route passes the gates, the real timetable (01:25 .. 04:50) instead of the demonstration schedule, the baked nav graph (a re-bake of `nav-*` brings the Troitsky pavement chains into the shipped nav), visual check in the browser (nothing here was looked at on a GPU).

### 13.10 The Palace Bridge (module `landmarksPalaceBridge`: TWO leaves that rise, and a bridge that the player can use while they do)

The Palace Bridge (Дворцовый мост, 254.5 m between the abutments, 27.7 m wide) is drawn by the `bridges` module (`bridges/palace.js`, since the first strip) and lies wholly inside the territory of `palace` (no registry row, no water row and no claim was changed: `REGISTRY.palace.play` holds the deck, `WATER.bridgeZones` 'palace' is `own`, `BRIDGE_SHADES` 'palace-deck' shades the whole deck, the ways `w362796524 / w362796525` stay in `GROUPS.bridges` key 'palace'). `landmarksPalaceBridge` adds the opening mechanism of its **central bascule span**: **two leaves**, one on each bascule pier, rise about 70 degrees about their own hinge lines in OPPOSITE senses and leave a 38 m gap in the middle (a 57.4 m span between the pier faces: leaf A 28.7 m from the hinge at u 96.5 to the midspan u 125.2, leaf B 28.7 m from the hinge at u 153.9 back to it; the counterweights hang on the pier side of the hinges). Like the Troitsky Bridge (13.8) it runs on a schedule of the game clock and the deck of each leaf is a MOVING, TILTING surface that the player and cars can stand on, drive on, and be carried by.

**The split (the cleanest of the two options).** The new module draws and moves exactly the span between the pier faces, `bridges/palace.js` draws everything else as before. It takes `env.leaves` (`bridges.js` passes `ctx.buildList.includes('landmarksPalaceBridge')`: the module is in the page's build list, so `?only=bridges` still draws the closed bridge) and then leaves out, between u 96.5 and 153.9, the deck, kerbs and markings, the girders (span 2 is drawn only from 85.75 to 96.5 and from 153.9 to 162.75: the same lens-shaped girder, cut at the hinges), the railing panels, posts and top rails, the two lamp standards (u 111 and 137.5) and all their colliders (the walk ramps are re-cut at the hinges, the railing ghost walls and the barrier kerbs likewise); its `deckAt` asks the module's service for that span (the live surface of a leaf, null in the gap: so `services.bridges.deckY`, which traffic, parked cars and the people nav read, sees the raised leaves and no phantom deck in the gap). The pier heads, their granite parapets (breakable bays), towers and bastions are untouched. **Without the module (the strip, the parked area, `?only=`) nothing changes: byte-identical.** Verified: a hash of the `bridges` module output (every vertex and index of every mesh, every physics item) is the same before and after the edit for the palace page and for the strip (the strip: also `tools/layout-golden.mjs --diff` identical, `layout-test` OK in the four areas); with the module on, `bridges` draws about 3,000 triangles and 81 physics items fewer.

| File | Content |
|---|---|
| `landmarksPalaceBridge.js` | `build(ctx)`: the leaves, the barriers, the service, the stop lines, the frame loop (clock step, leaf transforms and floors, barriers, toast, the people hooks, the carry / slip of the player or his car) |
| `landmarksPalaceBridge/frame.js` | PURE: the frame (that of `bridges/palace.js`), the cross-section, the closed deck profile `yDeck(u)` (`DECK_KEYS`, shared with `bridges/palace.js`), the two leaf descriptions (hinges U0 = 96.5 and U1 = 153.9, tips at the midspan UM), the gates, `WAY_IDS` |
| `landmarksPalaceBridge/schedule.js` | PURE: the tunable constants `CFG` and `createPalaceClock` |
| `landmarksPalaceBridge/service.js` | PURE: the bridge description for `lib/bascule.js`, the published service, the floor ramps, the bay specs (`makeBays`, `BAY_RAIL`, `BAY_KERB`, `KERB_PIECE`, `leafBayAt`), `stepWalker` / `stepCar` |
| `landmarksPalaceBridge/leaf.js` | the two moving groups (asphalt, granite barrier kerbs, asphalt pavements, markings, the fish-belly girders, soffit, six inner girders, the cast-iron railing posts and top rail, two lamp standards each, the counterweight), their floor ramps and the 88 BAYS of the rails and kerbs (breakable knock records that ride on them: see "Fences of the leaves" below) |
| `landmarksPalaceBridge/gates.js` | the barrier arms (8 booms: 2 gates x (2 carriageway + 2 pavement)) with red lamps; the booms themselves are solid, breakable knock records since 13.11 (`lib/bascule-gates.js`) |
| `landmarksPalaceBridge/groups.js` | claims NOTHING (`OWNS_GROUPS = []`, no `GROUPS` key; the ways stay with `bridges`); exports `WAY_IDS` |
| `lib/bascule.js` | PURE and generic (see below) |

`'landmarksPalaceBridge'` is in the palace `MODULES` after `bridges` and `landmarksTroitsky` (the stop lines need `streets`, the `deckAt` delegation needs `bridges`) and before `traffic` / `people` (they read it); `LABELS` has its loading text; `HOOKS.deckServices` lists it (`areas/north/hooks.js makeDeckServices`: cars and pedestrians ask it first); `src/world/lib/owns-table.js` is regenerated (`node tools/owns-table.mjs`: `"landmarksPalaceBridge": []`); `tools/check.mjs` has the BUDGET line 5.7 k (measured 4,936 triangles incl. the instanced rail panels).

**`src/world/lib/bascule.js`: the shared piece (new, used by this module only).** PURE: `createClock` (the warning -> opening -> hold -> closing -> clear state machine, `ease`), `makeLeaf` (a rigid leaf about a hinge line, pointing +u or -u: footprint, height, slope, carry, vertical speed), the footing rule (`slideAccel`, 35 / 25 degrees), `createBasculeService` (deckY, deckLift, stop flag, gates, pedestrian step test, onLeaf / leafAt, advance), `createRamp` / `placeRamp` (the per-frame floor), `leafBays` / `leafBayAt` (the layout of the rail panels and kerb pieces of a leaf and the world box of a bay that follows the surface; `leafBoxes`, the staircase of plain dynamic boxes, is gone), `addStopLines`, `stepWalker` / `stepCar` (carry and slip over any number of leaves), `hookPeople` (the four `people.sim.nav` wrappers), `sweepPeople`, `spanClear`. Its THREE.js companion is `src/world/lib/bascule-bays.js` (`createLeafBays`: the knock records, the world-frame instances and the per-angle re-pose of the bays of any leaf, either direction; `bascule.js` itself stays free of three.js and engine imports). **What `landmarksTroitsky` could later use from it** (nothing of it was touched): its `schedule.js createClock` and `ease` are the same code (the only addition here is `FIRST_NIGHT`, a separate first-opening time in the white night); `frame.js` leaf maths is `makeLeaf` with `dir` +1; `service.js` `createService`, `createRamps` / `placeRamps` (here one ramp per leaf: `createRamp` takes the width, a strip per call gives the three of Troitsky), `leafBays` / `createLeafBays` (its leaf.js builds the same 56 bays by hand), `addStopLines` (a `prefix` argument instead of the hard-coded `'troitsky-'`), `stepWalker` / `stepCar`, `bodyState`; and the `landmarksTroitsky.js` frame loop's `hookPeople` closure, straggler sweep and `ready()` check (about 60 lines) become three calls. `gates.js` and the leaf drawing stay per bridge.

**The schedule (`schedule.js CFG`, every number tunable at the top of the file; `?palacebridge=open|closed|auto`, default auto, also `service.setMode(m)`).** The same cycle as the Troitsky Bridge with its own numbers: **WARN** (>= 14 s: the barrier arms drop in 5 s, the STOP flag is up, the leaves have not moved; they wait, at most `WARN_MAX` = 75 s in all, until the span is clear of NPC vehicles and pedestrians) -> **OPENING** (`OPEN_T` = 70 s, `theta = 70 degrees * smootherstep(p)`, at most 1.87 degrees/s) -> **HOLD** (`HOLD` = 30 s; mode `open` stays open) -> **CLOSING** (70 s, the mirror; `setMode('closed')` while opening reverses `p` without a jump) -> **CLEAR** (6 s with the leaves down, then the arms lift and the flag drops). A cycle is 190 s. Both leaves share `p`, so they are mirror images at every moment. **Offset from the Troitsky Bridge so that the two never open together:** the first opening comes `FIRST_AT` = 160 s after the start (Troitsky 100 s; in the white night `FIRST_NIGHT` = 150 s against its 90 s), and the gaps are chosen so that the PERIOD is the same in both presets (day `DAY_GAP` 315 + 190 = 300 + 205 = 505 s; white night `NIGHT_GAP` 105 + 190 = 90 + 205 = 295 s): the Palace Bridge warns 60 s after the Troitsky Bridge every time, for as long as the preset does not change and neither file is retuned (`tools/palacebridge-test.mjs` simulates both real clocks for two hours in both presets and requires at least 30 s between their warnings: measured 60 s). The HUD toast "Дворцовый мост разводят · The Palace Bridge is opening" appears once per cycle, to a player within 700 m of the bridge. No sound, no flags.

**Published service** `ctx.services.landmarksPalaceBridge` (also `deckAt`, the Troitsky convention): `state()` -> `{ phase: 'closed' | 'opening' | 'open' | 'closing', stage ('idle' | 'warn' | 'opening' | 'hold' | 'closing' | 'clear'), angle (rad), t (0..1), stop, barrier (0 up .. 1 down), mode, cycles }`; `deckY(x, z)` (the asphalt level of the leaf that covers the point at the live angle; **`null` on the fixed spans** (the `bridges` module answers there, the service covers only u 96.5 .. 153.9) **and in the gap an open bridge leaves**); `deckLift(x, z)` (a pedestrian's feet above it: 0.12 on the pavement, 0.03 on the asphalt); `setMode`, `mode()`, `stop`, `theta`, `gates`, `stepBlocked(x0, z0, x1, z1)`, `nearGate`, `inSpan`, `inGated`, `onLeaf`, `leafAt` (0 = A, 1 = B, -1), `clock`, `leaf` (`outers`, `pivots`, `bodies`, `set(theta)`, `items()`), `B` (the bridge description), `frame`, `stats` (`tris`, `rails`, `arms`, `ramps`, `stopLines`, `swept`).

**Overhead wires and masts of the moving span (fix, 2026-10-25).** Nothing may hang over, or stand on, a span that is not closed. When the page builds `landmarksPalaceBridge`, `streets/wires.js` (zone `bridgeZones`: u 62.5 .. 185.5, between the last cross span of the fixed approach and the first of the other one, so the wires end at two masts in front of the barrier and never loose against a raised leaf) CLIPS every contact wire at the zone, draws no cross span, hanger or mast inside it (the two plain 4 m masts at the midspan, u about 124, that the gap fill used to put on the deck edges are gone with their colliders, so `water-reachable-test` now allows no plain solid on the span) and hands the clipped part to `ctx.services.streets.wires.cut.palace`. The module draws that part as one dynamic wire mesh (`lib/bridge-wires.js`: the street wire shader of `core/geo.js`, one shared `uRes`) that is visible only while the leaves are lowered to less than 3 degrees (`service.wires`, `stats.spanWires`) and hidden whenever a leaf is raised. The zone is gated by the build list, so the strip (no module) keeps every wire; the filter runs when the streets module builds, so the bake products carry no span masts. The closed bridge keeps its wires but no midspan cross span / masts (dropped, not attached to a leaf). Troitsky: no street wire crosses its leaf (`wires.cut.troitsky` is empty) and its contact pole on the leaf is part of the leaf group, so it rides the leaf. Look: `node tools/bridge-wires-smoke.mjs` (GPU job).

**The NPC world** (the Troitsky mechanism, through `lib/bascule.js`, no engine rewrite). *Cars:* `addStopLines` appends one stop line per carriageway lane toward the span (gate S at u = 79 before the first pier for the Admiralty-to-island carriageway `w362796525`, gate N at u = 171.5 beyond the second for `w362796524`; on the real lane graph of the page: 6 lines, 3 lanes x 2 ways) and wraps `streets.signalState / signalInfo`: the signals `palacebridge-s / -n` read `red` while the STOP flag is up. *Pedestrians:* `people/nav.js` already has the long-bridge band for `palace` (`LBAND`, fed by the `bridges` span entry); the module wraps `densityFactor` (between the gates a thinned zone while the flag is up), `fenceNear` / `segBlocked` (a step INTO the closed span through a gate line is a wall, a step out is free) and `reliefY` (the people on a moving leaf ride its surface); an NPC still on a leaf that is steeper than the walkers' slip angle is set down outside the gate of its hinge side (every 0.5 s). Known: the photo spot of the bridge (`B.at`, the upstream pavement at the bascule) is ON a leaf, so tourists who stand there (`stand` agents with no goal) are not drained by the thinned zone (in a 100 s run of the real page 2 or 3 of them stayed on the span until the leaves started); they ride the surface up to 35 degrees and are then set down at the gate (the clock does not wait for them longer than `WARN_MAX`). The PLAYER and his car are NOT stopped by the arms (no collider): he can walk or drive onto a leaf while it opens.

**A leaf as a surface.** Physics floor: ONE `walk ramp` item per leaf (the full 27.9 m width at the asphalt level, as the fixed deck's ramps: no pavement lift for the engine) whose `cx, cz, hd, yA, yB` are overwritten each frame by `placeRamp` (an item mutated at run time like a conveyor's `dir`; the footprint of the raised leaf lies inside the closed one, so the grid cells stay valid); `surfaceAt` / `core/player.js` / the car's `groundAt` (`deckY` under both axles; `drive/handling.js` unchanged) need no change. The closed leaf is the straight chord between the pier-face deck height and the midspan deck height (hinges 2.23 m and 2.27 m, tips 2.40 m; rest tilt 0.34 and 0.26 degrees): at most 3.5 cm under the old arched deck between the hinge and the tip, equal at both ends. **Colliders:** the two railings and the two granite barrier kerbs of each leaf are BAYS (a rail panel of 2.05 m, 14 per rail, 56 in all; a kerb piece of 3.6 m, 8 per kerb, 32 in all): each is a knock record (kind `fence`) whose collider is a DYNAMIC box (`ctx.physics.addDynamic` -> `refreshDynamic`), produced only while the player is within 160 m; the box follows the slope under its bay (`bascule.js leafBayAt`: the rise under a bay stays within 1.0 m for a panel up to 70 degrees and for a kerb piece up to the walker's footing limit of 35 degrees). **Breakability (the world rule, now kept on the moving leaves too, see "Fences of the leaves"):** the railing and the kerbs of the FIXED spans are breakable (bays, ghost bays); the rail panels and kerb pieces of a MOVING leaf are breakable knock records; the leaf's lamp standards have no collider (nothing to hit); the gate arms and posts have none. **Carry:** each frame the module sets a player (or his car) that stands on a leaf where the rotating leaf carries him (`carryDu`: a material point moves toward its hinge as the leaf rises) and on its surface: continuous contact, the vertical speed handed over is `(distance from the hinge) * dTheta/dt` <= 0.81 m/s at the free end (1.87 degrees/s x 28.7 m). **Slip rule** (as 13.8): beyond a slope of **35 degrees (walker)** / **25 degrees (car)** a body slides down toward ITS leaf's hinge: `a = g (sin phi - tan(limit) cos phi)`, along-slope speed capped at 7 m/s (walker) / 16 m/s (car); a walker's own uphill step of the frame is taken back, a car's velocity along u is held at or below the slide; the slide ends on the pier (flat), and a body that leaves the pier edge into the gap falls to the river bed (water is reachable: no special handling). They begin at theta = 34.7 degrees (walker) and 24.7 degrees (car).

**Fences of the leaves (the world rule of 13.7 on a moving surface; the Troitsky protocol of 13.8, now in `lib/bascule.js` + `lib/bascule-bays.js` for any leaf).** Every rail panel (60 kg, breaks at 12 km/h like the fixed panels of the bridge) and every barrier kerb piece (granite, 900 kg, 30 km/h) of both leaves is a knock record of its own, built by `createLeafBays` from `leafBays(leaf, spec)`: its instance lives in a WORLD-frame knock mesh (`lib/knock-inst.js`, one per 384 m cell and part; not a child of the moving group) that `leaf.set(theta)` re-poses whenever the angle changes (the record origin, the rest offsets `off` / `q` / `s` of its part and the instance matrix are written from the leaf transform, so a hit at ANY angle breaks the bay from the pose it has now: it topples about its foot, sparks and clank, a bay over the open river sinks and is gone), and its collider is the record's own `src` used as a dynamic box (the protocol of `drive/collision.js`: a car hit goes through the ordinary `invMassRatio` / `applyCarImpulse`, nothing leaf-specific). The deck strip under the kerb pieces, the posts and the top rail strip of the railing stay drawn when a bay is gone, as on every bridge. **The rule for what is left lying** is the Troitsky one: the debris of a broken bay lies where it fell (a rest box of at most 0.42 m, a car drives over it) only while the leaves lie closed; as soon as they move (theta > 0.001) it is swept away (rest box removed, instance hidden); a broken bay is never rebuilt. Without `ctx.knock` (`tools/check.mjs` of the module alone) the rail panels ride in the moving group as one instanced mesh, the kerb is part of the moving mesh and the bays are plain dynamic boxes (the old path). **A car needs about 65 km/h** to go through the railing of a leaf from the roadway: the pavement between the kerb and the railing is only 2.4 m wide, so the car loses its speed on the 900 kg kerb piece first (the kerb pieces are 3.6 m, wider than a car, so one hit meets one piece; 2.4 m pieces were tried and held the car, which straddled two of them). **Per-frame cost:** `leaf.set` runs only when the angle changed (the module's frame loop calls it for `r.th1 !== r.th0`), uses module scratch objects and allocates nothing; `leafBayAt` writes into one scratch object (the old `leafBoxes` called `toWorld` without an `out` argument: one object per box per frame, and rebuilt every box 60 times a second while the leaves lay); `svc.advance` returns ONE reused result object and `clock.takeEvents()` a shared frozen empty list in the frames without an event; `placeRamp` writes into a scratch point; the physics provider appends the existing box objects and is marked `stableBoxes`, so `core/physics.js refreshDynamic` keeps one OBB per box between calls and rewrites it in place only when the box moved (a lying leaf: nothing is allocated, nothing is recomputed; the other providers keep the old path). Known item that is NOT the leaves': the streets module keeps two tram contact-wire masts (plain cylinders, 4 m tall) of the bridge deck merged and unbreakable at the middle of the span (u about 124, the rail line); `tools/water-reachable-test.mjs` names them, the audit allows exactly those two. The fixed spans' barrier kerbs are still plain solid boxes (`bridges/palace.js`, 8 m long, 0.3 m wide), not part of this change.

**Tests (CPU only).** `npm run palacebridge` (`node tools/area.mjs palace tools/palacebridge-test.mjs`, about 1,600 checks, 6 s): the wiring and the claim; the kinematics of both leaves (hinges on the pier faces, closed = the deck profile within 4 cm, the tips meet, heights rise, slopes, carry, opposite senses, the 38 m gap, vertical speed <= 1.3 m/s, the footing rule); the schedule (the flag is up >= 14 s before the leaves move and drops after they lie and the arms are up, theta is continuous, the easing, the modes, the night rule, the offset from the Troitsky clock); the service (`deckY` = the profile on the closed bascule span, continuous in space and time, the live leaves at several angles, `null` in the gap and on the fixed spans); the stop lines on the real ways and the pedestrian step test, the people hooks, the set-down; the REAL `bridges` + module built headless (`tools/tauride/lib/stub-env.mjs`): the whole closed deck equals the old profile through `services.bridges.deckY` with no hole, the open bridge has no deck in the gap and the live leaves in `services.bridges.deckY`, the transform chain of both leaves (the senses), the physics floor equals `deckY` at every angle and the old ramps of the fixed spans are unchanged, the dynamic rails follow both leaves, the real `Player` on each leaf through a cycle (contact, carry, slip), the module's own frame loop carrying a walker, a walker dropped into the gap, the real `drive/handling.js` car on each leaf (carried near the hinge, slips at 25 degrees, drives onto a closing leaf, crosses the closed span without a bump); section 7: every bay of both leaves is a knock record of kind `fence` with the record's own box and no static collider, no plain solid stands on the span, the bays (instance, record origin, rest offsets, far end, box) sit on their leaf at six angles in both senses, `refreshDynamic` hands out the same collider objects while the leaves rest, a car at 65 km/h breaks a kerb piece and rail panels of the CLOSED bridge on each leaf and each side and falls into the Neva while a car at 8 km/h is held by the kerb, the rule for what lies on a moving leaf (hit toward the roadway lies low, toward the water sinks, the debris is swept when the leaf moves, a bay hit at 23 degrees breaks from its raised pose, nothing broken comes back when the leaf closes, on both leaves) and a walker leaving the closed leaf through the gap of a broken panel while an intact neighbour holds him. Also `node tools/area.mjs palace tools/check.mjs landmarksPalaceBridge` (and `--area-modules`: the whole page builds, 6 stop lines on the real lanes, the people nav is hooked), `npm run golden`, `npm run layout`. **Known red, not this module:** the pitch-following check of the car on a leaf (`the body pitches with the slope`, once per leaf: 2 checks) fails the way the Troitsky one does: `drive/handling.js` ignores an axle that is more than `DROP_MAX` = 0.45 m above or below the middle (a kerb rule of the water work), so the car's pitch follows the slope only up to 19 degrees; the check asserts the fixed contract (the pitch follows the slope all the way to the 25-degree slip) and turns green with the `handling.js` fix. The closing-leaf contact check passes here (the car creeps at 9 km/h onto the leaf and stops 14 m from the hinge; in `troitsky-test.mjs` the car is under full throttle). `npm run water` (`tools/water-reachable-test.mjs`, 17 green on the palace row): its audit "6 (the Palace Bridge leaves)" requires the 88 bays to be fence knock records with a dynamic box and no plain solid on the span between the hinges (the two streets contact-wire masts excepted, see above).

**Not done / follow-ups:** sound (the bell, the leaf drive), boats passing through the open span (the water under the gap is the Neva as before), navigation lights and the night illumination of the raised leaves (the lamps on a leaf are not in `services.bridges.masts`), a visible counterweight pit (the weights swing down inside the pier body, hidden), the walkers' horizontal carry for NPC people (they ride the surface but are not carried), the real timetable (01:25 .. 04:50) instead of the demonstration schedule, the bake (every file under `src/` is in the bake revision: the palace bake is stale until it is rebuilt), the baked `nav-*` graph (the long-bridge chains were cut with the closed deck: the leaves lie at the same heights, so nothing moves in it), visual check in the browser (nothing here was looked at on a GPU).

## 14. Seamless hand-off (edge gates, freeze veil)

Entering a neighbour area must feel like one scene: no question, no card. When the player reaches the edge of the area the frame **freezes, blurs a little and a single progress bar** fills in the middle of the screen while the next page loads; when the new area is ready the veil fades into the live scene and he walks on from the same pose (heading, time of day, car, mute). Code: `src/core/gates.js` (runtime-only, so none of it enters the bake revision; `index.html` and `src/main.js` do, the next rebake covers them). Tests: `tools/gates-test.mjs`, `tools/handoff-test.mjs` (CPU).

### 14.1 The edge gate row

```js
{ id: 'e1', kind: 'edge', from: 'strip', to: 'north', zone: 40 /* optional, m, walking */, reverse: 'e2' }
```

No `seg`, no `inward`, no `arrive`: the territories come from `REGISTRY[id].play` (a union of rectangles, street frame, x east, z south) and overlap their neighbour's by about one block (section 12). The page's own `inPlay` (layout.js) stands in for FROM's territory (`createGates` dep `inPlay`; `createGateMachine` options `rectsOf` / `inFrom` for tests). Several edge gates may leave one area (one per neighbour; the first match wins). Border (`seg` / `inward`) and `door` gates keep working; only their UI changed (they no longer ask). `inUnion(rects, x, z, margin)` has the semantics of layout.js `_inUnion` (inside, at least `margin` m from the union's boundary; a negative margin also takes the outside band).

### 14.2 The trigger rule (per frame, no allocation)

With p the player's position (car: the car's), in FROM's territory, and depth = `zone` (40 m) for a walker, `depthFor(mode, v)` (the vehicle formula: 40..160 m) for a car or flyer: the gate triggers when for one of the 8 directions (every 45 degrees, N = -z, clockwise) the probe q = p + depth * dir is **outside FROM's territory and inside TO's by at least 60 m** (`edgeIn`; so the arrival is never at the destination's rim), and the player is moving toward q: his velocity component along dir is >= `approachV` (0.5 m/s) **and** >= 0.92 of his speed (`edgeAlign`: only the octant he heads in counts, so walking ALONG an edge triggers nothing), or he stands within `nearD` (8 m) of the edge in that direction. The rest is the machine of the border gates: no trigger in the first `graceS` (6 s) after an arrival, `cooldown(g)` 30 s after a failed preflight, `markOpen(g)` while a travel runs, `decline(g)` (only with `?gateprompt=1`) 45 s and until the player has left the zone by 20 m, blocked while a panel is open, indoors, underground, riding, travelling.

The arrival has no rectangle: the player lands at the **same x, z, yaw, pitch**; `resolveArrival` only moves him (the ring search, 3..60 m, no clamp) when something blocks it, a car needs a lane within 30 degrees of its heading. The URL protocol (`?cam&fly&tod&look&ho`) is unchanged.

### 13.11 Car stunts on the bascule bridges (the owner's request of 2026-10-25: a leaf is a RAMP, the barriers are real)

**What the player meets.** (The boom paragraphs of this section - 15 km/h break speed, 40 kg, one record per boom - are superseded by 15.x: light two-piece booms that give way at any speed.) The red-and-white booms that drop across the road when a bridge is about to open are now SOLID and BREAKABLE. A car that touches a lowered boom below 15 km/h is stopped by it; above that the boom breaks off at its middle, topples, and lies on the road as a low rest box (a car drives over it, like any fence of the world, 13.7); a broken boom stays broken until the NEXT cycle starts, when the module stands it up again. The leaf of an opening bridge is a RAMP for a car up to 45 degrees (it was 25: a car slid back from there): the car climbs the rising leaf if its speed and power allow, leaves the TIP with its full velocity vector and FLIES across the gap on a gravity arc, with the nose dipping along the velocity; it lands on the far leaf's tilted deck, on the fixed deck beyond, or it falls short into the Neva or hits the face of the far leaf, with damage from the speed into the surface and the pitch error. The Palace Bridge's open gap is ~38 m between the tips at 70 degrees, 5 m at 25 degrees, 7.7 m at 30: a car at 25 m/s clears the whole bridge, a car at 12 m/s comes down on the far leaf, a car at 8 m/s falls into the gap.

**The booms (`lib/bascule-gates.js`, three.js, new; `landmarksPalaceBridge/gates.js` and `landmarksTroitsky/gates.js` use it).** `createBarrierArms(ctx, { name, toWorld, arms, mats })`: every boom is ONE knock record (kind `fence`, `anchored: 15` km/h of the car at contact = ARM_BREAK, originY 0.95) whose three instance parts (red stripes, white stripes, the counterweight stub; `knockInstances`, world-frame meshes) are re-posed by `set(barrier)` while the boom swings (psi = (1 - barrier) x 72 degrees about the post), and whose collider is the record's own DYNAMIC box (`rec.src`, the protocol of `lib/bascule-bays.js`: a `stableBoxes` provider on `ctx.physics.addDynamic`, no per-frame allocation) that exists only while the boom is within BOX_PSI = 20 degrees of the road (a raised boom has no box); the box follows the boom (length x cos psi, from the foot to the tip height + 0.15 m). The record stands at the middle of the lowered boom: a hit topples it about that point, `knock()` lays the rest box (<= 0.42 m). `restore()` (the module calls it on the clock's `warn` event) re-arms every broken record through the new `ctx.knock.restore(record)` (core/knock.js: a flying body is dropped, the rest box removed, hidden parts shown; `update` skips a finished body) and re-poses it. The NPC stop logic (stop lines, `stepBlocked` for pedestrians, `nearGate`) is untouched. The booms block the WALKING player too (as any box does): a lowered boom is a wall for him; the booms are up outside the warning cycle.

**The ballistic phase (`drive/handling.js`, the exported `STUNT` table holds every number).** Before, a car that left the ground started its fall with zero vertical speed and kept steering and gripping in the air. Now `settle()` is the whole vertical model of a car that is not hopping:
* *Take-off* (`launch`): when the surface under the centre drops by more than DROP_MAX = 0.45 m, the car keeps its horizontal velocity untouched and gets vy = vF tan(slopeOn) + deckVy, where slopeOn = the last slope under BOTH axles (`car.slopeOn`, kept while no axle is over an edge) and deckVy = the vertical speed of the surface itself (a rising leaf adds its lift: `deckVy(x, z)` of the deck services, `lib/bascule.js` / Troitsky `service.js`: dir (u - uHinge) dTheta/dt; a real moving ramp); the pitch starts at slopeOn with the lift-off rate -LIFT_ALPHA (3.9 rad/s^2) x half the wheelbase / vh (the rear axle's last contact: g cos(slope) a / (k^2 + a^2)), the roll rate from the steering (ROLL0 0.35 rad/s at a full steer and 20 m/s). `car.flight` records the take-off (x0, y0, vh0, vy0) and the touchdown.
* *Flight* (`car.fall`): gravity FALL_G = 12 m/s^2 (the same gravity as the walker and the knock bodies; the owner's 9.81 would be the only odd one in the game), NO air drag, no drive / brakes / grip (the horizontal speed is kept exactly: the test pins 5e-11 m), steering x 0.15; the attitude `flightAttitude`: a second-order spring toward the velocity direction atan2(vy, vh) (AIR_KP 2.4 1/s^2, AIR_KD 1.7 1/s: the nose dips as the arc turns down; no instant snap), the roll relaxes (ROLL_KP 6, ROLL_KD 2.6), pitch limited to 1.3 rad. Integration is semi-implicit (v -= g h, y += v h per substep): the arc is y0 + vy0 t - g t^2 / 2 - g h t / 2 exactly.
* *Catching* a flying car: a deck catches the wheels only within 0.35 m under its surface (`CATCH`; the ground rule is 1.2 m); lower, the car meets the FACE of the leaf: the leaf's floor ramp item has a solid depth of 2.4 m (Palace `BRIDGE.thick`) / 2.0 m (Troitsky `THICK`), `collision.js` does the rest (the hit event, the damage, the push-out). Nothing in the air is carried by a leaf: `stepCar` returns at once for `car.fall` / `car.air`.
* *Touchdown* (`land`): the slope of the surface under the axles (an axle that is on the same deck counts; an edge counts flat), the speed INTO the surface relative to the moving deck, vn = max(0, vF sin s - vyRel cos s); the car keeps the tangential speed (no bounce: it rolls on along the surface, so a landing on a downhill slope costs little and a flat one costs the whole fall); the damage is the usual `hit` event of (vEff - 4.2) x 1.6 with vEff = vn + LAND_PITCH_K (10) x max(0, |pitch - slope| - 0.15 rad) x min(1, vh / 12): the nose or the tail lands first (the event's local point is the front or the rear), drive/damage.js does the rest ((v - 3) / 45 of a zone per hit).
* *Climbing*: on the ground a slope steeper than 7 degrees (full from 11) pulls the car back along it: a = -g sin(s) cos(s) of horizontal speed (energy: v^2 >= 2 g h_gain, or the engine's own 15.5 m/s^2 x (1 - (v / vmax)^1.7), which climbs 45 degrees at low speed: tan = 1 = the tyre friction); a car AT REST holds up to 25 degrees (the handbrake up to 45), steeper it rolls back. `lib/bascule.js CAR_SLIP_DEG = 45` (Troitsky `frame.js` too): beyond it the car slips down the leaf toward the hinge as before, and the FOOT of such a leaf is a wall (`footWall`: a car whose centre comes within 2 m of the hinge line, moving toward it, is put back, loses its speed toward the leaf with a 15 % bounce and takes a `hit` through `car.pending`, which `handling.js` moves to `events` at the next step: a 50-degree leaf is not a ramp: the car crashes into it). The carry of a car that stands on a closing or rising leaf is unchanged: continuous contact, the vertical speed is the leaf's (<= 1.76 m/s).
* *Axle probes*: `step()` reads the axle heights with a deck reference 1.4 m above the car (a leaf raised by 45 degrees rises 1.8 m over the wheelbase); the old reference (1.2 m under the surface) lost the front axle on slopes steeper than ~42 degrees and tilted the car by half the slope (the "DROP_MAX merge problem" that two old bridge tests mention is gone: they pass now).

| constant | value | where |
|---|---|---|
| gravity in flight | 12 m/s^2 (FALL_G) | handling.js |
| DROP_MAX / CATCH tolerance | 0.45 m / 0.35 m | handling.js |
| slope gravity | from 7 degrees, full from 11; hold at rest 25 (handbrake 45) | handling.js |
| AIR_KP, AIR_KD | 2.4 1/s^2, 1.7 1/s | handling.js |
| LIFT_ALPHA, ROLL0 | 3.9 rad/s^2, 0.35 rad/s | handling.js |
| LAND_PITCH_TOL, LAND_PITCH_K | 0.15 rad, 10 m/s per rad | handling.js |
| CAR_SLIP_DEG / WALK_SLIP_DEG | 45 / 35 degrees | lib/bascule.js, Troitsky frame.js |
| boom: break speed, box limit, raised angle | see 15.x Bridge fixes (any car contact breaks a half) | lib/bascule-gates.js |

**What the numbers do (`npm run bridgejump`, 82 checks, ~7 s).** Palace, leaf at 25 degrees: a car at 25 m/s leaves the tip at 14.4 m with vh 25.0 / vy 11.7 m/s, flies 2.7 s, lands 62 m beyond the far leaf's tip projection on the island road with a 20.4 m/s impact and a nose-down error of 23 degrees (damage 0.60 of a zone); at 12.5 m/s from 3 m before the tip it lands ON the far leaf's deck (a downhill slope of -24.9 degrees, 0.00 damage); at 30 degrees and 8 m/s it comes down 2.6 m short and ends in the Neva (damage 0.72). Troitsky (one 43 m leaf, tip 18.6 m up at 25 degrees): 25 m/s lands on the fixed deck beyond the pier; the failing case needs a steep leaf (44 degrees, 4 m/s: 3.7 m short, in the water; the gap of the single leaf is only 4 m at 25 degrees, so a slow car that does reach the tip lands on the fixed deck beyond from 18 m up).

**Tests.** `tools/bridge-jump-test.mjs` (npm `bridgejump`: `node tools/area.mjs palace tools/bridge-jump-test.mjs`): the real modules and the real handling on both bridges, the clock frozen at a held angle (`Object.defineProperty` on the clock's `theta` / `omega`): (a) 25 m/s up a leaf at 20 / 25 / 30 degrees: take-off numbers, a landing on the far side with finite damage (both Palace leaves, the Troitsky leaf); (a2) 12.5 m/s lands on the far leaf's tilted deck; (b) the short fall into the water with damage; (c) the arc: y within 10 cm of y0 + vy0 t - g t^2 / 2 (exact once the integrator's g h t / 2 is removed), the horizontal distance within 2 cm of vh t, no change of the horizontal speed, the apex, the attitude (starts at the slope, dips with the velocity); (d) a car at 5 km/h stopped by a lowered boom (it stands), at 45 km/h breaks it (rest box), it stays broken after the cycle, the next `warn` restores it, raised booms have no box; (e) a car with the handbrake on a closing leaf is carried (contact 0.000 m, <= 0.22 m/s, no take-off); (f) a 50-degree leaf is a wall, a coasting 8 m/s car stalls on a 28-degree leaf and rolls back. The old rules that the stunts change ("a car slips beyond 25 degrees", "the arms are a picture") were updated in `tools/palacebridge-test.mjs` and `tools/troitsky-test.mjs` (45 degrees; the parked car of the carry test has its handbrake on; the arm height is read back from the instances); everything else in them (1,607 / 12,440 checks), `water-reachable-test` and the drive and knock tests pass unchanged.

**Known limits.** The flight is a point mass with an attitude (no wheel / suspension model, no bounce on touchdown, no spin about the vertical axis); the leaf's face is the ramp item's box (2.4 m / 2.0 m deep), not the girder's real profile; a flying car is not carried by a moving leaf and the leaf's lift at the TIP is at most 1.76 m/s; a walker is stopped by a lowered boom (the booms are boxes for everybody; the walking player cannot break one).

### 14.3 The veil flow

1. **Trigger** (an edge or border gate, E at a door gate, a pick from the HUD list of CROSS_PLACES): `go()` runs at once. The sim and the input are frozen (`ctx.travelPending`, `player.inputLock`). The preflight (HEAD of the page + manifest revision) is silent; a failure shows a short toast, starts the cooldown and unfreezes.
2. **Freeze.** `ctx.captureFrame()` (main.js) is answered by the next `frame()` right after `pipeline.render`, in the same task (the WebGL drawing buffer is not preserved): `grabFrame` draws the canvas into a 2D canvas of at most 1280 px width and takes a JPEG (quality 0.72); an all-black frame is refused. Without a frame (a hidden tab: 0.8 s time-out, a failure) the veil is plain dark.
3. **Veil on the origin page.** `#seam` (index.html: `.seam-snap`, `.seam-shade`, a thin rounded glowing track with a gradient fill in the gold accent #f2c14e, a shimmer, a tabular percentage; no other text) fades in: blur 0 -> 10 px and a vignette over 350 ms (`prefers-reduced-motion`: no shimmer or glow animation, the global rule drops the transitions). The snapshot and facts go to `sessionStorage` (below), then `api.navigate` (`location.replace`). Nothing of the destination is prefetched. If the new page has not committed in 25 s the travel is cancelled (`stop()`, toast, cooldown), the game goes on.
4. **Veil on the destination page.** The head carries a tiny script that sees `ho=` in the URL and sets `html.seam-in` BEFORE the first paint; the head CSS then hides `#intro` and darkens the page (`#0b1230`), whatever else is late. The veil markup and its inline script are the FIRST thing in `<body>` (before the canvas and the splash): the script reads the stored entry (fresh: less than 60 s old, an image, written for THIS area) and paints the same veil at once (blurred picture, the bar at the entry's `p`, see 14.4); without an entry (private mode, blocked storage, a bookmarked URL with `ho`) the veil is plain dark, never the splash. `setProgress` drives the bar with the loader's 0..1.
5. **Start by itself.** When the scene is built a page with a valid hand-off calls `start(true)` (no "Continue", no pointer lock request, no area toast; `applyHandoffAtStart` as before: mute, HUD, the car), two frames later `seam.finish()`: the blur relaxes while the picture cross-fades into the live frame (700 ms), the element is removed and the stored entry dropped. The first key / pointer resumes a suspended AudioContext (`audio.start()` resumes); the first click on the canvas takes the pointer lock; walking with the keys works at once. A `ho` that does not validate is no hand-off: the veil goes and the splash returns. A fatal error (`piter-fatal`) removes the veil; the 45 s loading watchdog does NOT (it puts its notice and a Reload button under the bar, inside the veil). A normal visit (no `ho`) is untouched: the splash with "В Питер".

**The preloader rule (the owner, 2026-10-25).** When the player changes areas he sees ONE minimalistic preloader and then the new scene: the blurred frozen frame with the single centred progress bar, from the freeze on the origin page to the first live frame on the destination. The splash card (`#intro`: title, facts, the Continue button, its own bar), the pre-start warning card of `core/boot-guard.js` and any other loading screen must never appear in between. Mechanics that hold it: (1) `html.seam-in` is set by a head script (placed BEFORE the render-blocking Google Fonts sheet: a classic script waits for the stylesheets before it, so the body script would otherwise run only after a slow sheet) that also reads the stored entry into the custom properties `--seam-img` / `--seam-p`; the head CSS hides `#intro`, darkens the page and draws the veil's end state (blurred picture, bar at `--seam-p`, label hidden until the veil script ran) from those properties alone, so even a frame painted before the body script is the right veil, not a bare dark one; (2) the veil markup and its inline script are the first thing of `<body>`; (3) the veil is never torn down by a timer: the loading watchdog (45 s without progress) shows its notice and Reload button INSIDE the veil (`.seam-note`); the old behaviour removed the veil and `seam-in`, which showed the splash and, because `seamless` is read when the build ends, turned the arrival into a page waiting for a click on Continue; (4) `main.js` skips `guard.gate()` (the phone warning card, z-index above the veil) on a hand-off page unless a real crash loop is detected; (5) the bar is continuous: the origin page raises it to `SEAM_LEAD` and stores it as `p`, the destination starts there and maps the real progress onto `p..1`; the fill has no CSS transition, the fill and the label are drawn from the same eased value (the former 0.45 s transition made the bar lag behind its label); (6) a bad `ho` (not a hand-off) is the only way back to the splash. Proof: `node tools/edge-smoke.mjs --net 8000,80 --cpu 3 --ret` records EVERY animation frame of the destination document from its creation (`#intro` visible, veil shown, bar and label) and fails on any frame with the splash, on a frame before the game runs without the veil, on a bar that goes backwards or apart from its label; `--stall 60000` holds the destination's first bake request past the watchdog; `--block-storage` removes sessionStorage; `--mode walk`, `--ret` (the way back), `--cpu`, `--fonts-delay` (the render-blocking Google Fonts sheet), `--remote <url>` (a deployed site) are the other knobs.

### 14.4 Storage and switches

* `sessionStorage['n4spb.seam']` = `{ img: <data: URL of the JPEG>, w, h, t: Date.now(), from, to, p }` (`p` = where the origin page left the bar, `SEAM_LEAD` 0.12), written by the origin page, read by the destination's inline script and cleared at `finish()`. Every access is wrapped: a blocked or full storage only costs the picture (plain veil).
* `?gateprompt=1` (development) restores the old passive chip (#gatechip, key N) and the `ui.confirm` question; `Загрузить` runs the same `go()`. Default: no question, no chip.
* `?devgates=1` keeps g0 (the strip loops back to the same page), the quickest way to see the whole flow; `?gates=0` / `?gates=1` as before.

## 15.x Bridge fixes (branch fix-bridges, the owner's reports of 2026-10-26): undecked OSM bridges, light two-piece barrier booms

**(1) "Driving a car on a bridge you fall through it" (pose -827.9, -2.3, -1715.9).** The point lies on the **Прачечный мост (Prachechny Bridge)**, OSM `w548349392` + two footways, over the Fontanka at its mouth (Palace-Liteyny). It is none of the module bridges (bridges / northBridges / landmarksTroitsky / landmarksPalaceBridge) and not a 1.5.0 stunt problem: **nothing carried a car there**. The `bridges` module draws only the sites of `bridges/sites.js`; the quay's "invisible fallback deck" exists only for `BRIDGE_ZONES` rows, and the Palace area had no row for the mapped bridges that the re-cut brought in; since water is reachable (no quay wall) the road simply led off the bank into the trench (`groundAt` = the bed, -4.6 m). The same hole was under the **Верхний Лебяжий мост** (Upper Swan, `w548349395`, the Swan Canal at the Neva) and the **2-й Инженерный мост** (`w466200209`, the castle moat); an audit of every named drivable OSM bridge of the area against the car's real ground chain found no other fall (the Triple Bridge is a walk-box deck of landmarksCastle, the module bridges are carried).
Fix: new **floor-only rows** in `BRIDGE_ZONES` (`floor: true`; palace `area.js`: `prachechny`, `swan-upper`, `eng2`; centre, bearing, width = road + footways + 0.6 m, length = bank to bank, taken from the OSM ways): `river.js bridgeZones()` skips them (no quay gap, no water shade, nothing else changes), `floorZones()` lists them, `quay.js` lays **one invisible walk box at y 0 (bottom -1.2 m)** per row, exactly the oriented box of the row. A floor is not a wall: nothing to break; its edges are open, a car that drives off the side still falls into the water (water stays reachable). Further undecked bridges of any area are one row each. Footbridges (`w1177904549`, `w1177904528`, `w1178011610`, `w1178011620`, the pier walk `w185004121`) are not covered (walkers wade; no car).
Checks: `tools/water-reachable-test.mjs` section **9** (both areas): every named drivable OSM bridge way over water keeps the car's ground at the road level on every water cell of the way (33 ways in the Palace area; before the fix Prachechny, Upper Swan and 2nd Engineer failed), and a real car (`createHandling`) drives the Prachechny Bridge both ways at 6 and 12 m/s without dropping into the trench. The check-4 car-through-a-fence run lifts the floors out for its run (`ONLY=<text>` runs a subset of the checks).

**(2) "The red-white barriers are too heavy, hard to drive through: make it lighter and breakable in half on car collision" (bascule booms, 13.11).** The booms were ONE anchored record per boom (40 kg, break speed 15 km/h): below 15 km/h a rigid wall (the car stopped dead), above it the boom "toppled" about its middle about the axis along itself (it never really fell) and left a 1 m rest box. Now `lib/bascule-gates.js`: **every boom is two halves** (post half, outer half), each a light knock record (`ARM_MASS` 8 kg, not anchored, `hitK` 0.05, `minDv` 0.1) with a **`beam`** (core/knock.js): it gives way to ANY car at any speed (the car keeps 99 %+ of its speed at 5, 15, 25, 45 km/h; pitch <= 0.07 rad, roll 0; no damage worth the name), and the boom **breaks in half**: the hit half and its partner (`ctx.knock.snap`, called from `onKnock`) break together. The post half **swings open about its post** like a gate pushed open (yaw toward the throw, at most until it lies along it, and droops until its free end lies on the road); the outer half is **thrown along the hit, falls flat, spins a little and lies**. Both end as thin rest boxes (0.35 m, a car drives over them); the NPC stop logic, the stunt ramp of the leaf, `restore()` on the next `warn` (both halves stand again, rest boxes removed, instances re-posed), the raised booms without a collider are unchanged. `arms[i].pieces = [post half, outer half]` (`rec`, `box`, `refs`), `arms[i].state` = `'stand'` / `'broken'`.
Checks: `tools/bridge-jump-test.mjs` (d): 136 checks (was 82): at 5 / 15 / 25 / 45 km/h the car passes the lowered boom (its speed before / in / after the boom, pitch, roll), the boom is in two pieces (both `rest`, two different rest boxes <= 0.5 m thick, the post half swung away > 2 m and lies on the road, the outer half too), the restored boom hangs at the old place again; `palacebridge-test` (1,607) and `troitsky-test` (12,440) pass unchanged.
**By hand:** Palace Bridge (`?palacebridge=open`) or Troitsky Bridge (`?troitsky=open`): wait ~5 s until the red-white booms are down across the carriageway (the warning phase, before the leaves rise) - drive into a lowered boom at 5, 20 or 40 km/h: the car goes through without being slowed, the boom snaps in two (the half at the post swings aside, the other half drops ahead of the car). For the Fontanka bridge: drive a car over the Prachechny Bridge near x -829, z -1716 (the Summer Garden end of the Fontanka).

## 15.wasted Junked car: the "Потрачено" label

* `src/world/drive/wasted.js` (wired in `drive.js`): the step the player's car reaches 100% body damage (`damage.amount >= 0.999`, the worst zone crushed through) shows a centred red GTA-style label with the exact text "Потрачено" for 5 s of game time (DOM/CSS, `pointer-events: none`, fade-in with a scale-in, grey vignette, fade-out), once per wreck event, plus a toast "Нажмите J — ремонт".
* From that moment `wasted.gate()` replaces the input with the held-car input (`holdInput`): gas, steering, nitro and hops are ignored, the car brakes to a standstill and never reverses. E (exit), the horn and J / the Repair buttons work as before; `api.repair()` has no rule against a wrecked car (only "stop first"), the repair lifts the hold and removes a running label at once.
* The label is also removed on exit, on the R respawn (`place`), and is not shown when a wrecked car is entered or arrives through a hand-off (the state is adopted silently, the car stays immobile).
* Check: `node tools/drive-wasted-test.mjs` (CPU). By hand: drive at full speed into a wall three or four times.

## 15.x Quay holes at the Anichkov landing stage (branch fix-quay-holes, 2026-10-26)

Two owner reports on the west bank of the Fontanka (street frame x east, z south; pose x,y,z,yaw,pitch with yaw 0 = north, positive = counter-clockwise, so yaw 274 looks EAST).
* **(a) the landing stage flight at (-452.9, -166.3)** (OSM pier of the Anichkov stage; `quay.stairs[0]` of the Fontanka, drawn since the first quay code, never walkable): the railing ran across the flight and the flight had no floors. Now `OPEN_FONTANKA_STAIRS` in `src/world/environment/quay.js` opens it: a gap of +-1.2 m in the railing (`inGate` / `inOpenGate`: no post, no bay spans it; the posts at u = +-1.45 frame it, 1.8 m clear), walk boxes for the top landing (0.5 m under the pavement), a hidden sill (two 0.25 m steps back up to the street), the 11 treads (`addStairs`), the lower landing and the pontoon deck of landmarksC/boats.js (`PONTOON`, deck top -2.72). Frame: p0 = (-452.90, -166.33), descent direction t = (0.183, -0.983) (north-north-east along the wall), the water is at (+0.983, +0.183); the pontoon (15 x 3 m) lies from 9 m to 24 m along t, its middle at about (-448.4, -182.3), its outer rail at v = 3.0 m (at u = 9 that is (-448.3, -174.6); a boat moored 2-4 m beyond it lies at v 5 .. 7, x about -447 .. -445). The flight record is `quay.allStairs[i]` with `walk: true, topY, pontoonTop, gate`.
* **(b) the pose (-463.3, 1.5, -121.9, 634.4 = 274.4 deg, -39.5)** stands on the street 1.7 m west of the quay railing and looks east-down at it: the railing stopped one bay (3.6 m) short of the Anichkov Bridge railing (the posts inside the deck polygon + 1.2 m are left out, the bridge rail starts where the first of them stood): a real hole beside the bridge head. Fixed generally: the first / last post of the deck zone is the end of a closing bay (`postAt` in quay.js), so the four heads of the bridge on the Fontanka banks are closed (-461.62,-123.72 -> -462.35,-120.16 and the three others). Same bank as (a), 48 m north of it.
* Check: `node tools/area.mjs palace tools/water-reachable-test.mjs` (section 7b: the gate is free, the railing stands beside it, a walker goes street -> landing -> flight -> pontoon dry and back, the four bridge heads are continuous). The parked strip shares quay.js and gets the same fix at the same places; smolny is unchanged.

## 15.x The motor boat (the first water transport; branch `feat-boat`)

The owner (2026-10-26): a motor boat that moves on the water, parked near the Anichkov Bridge, that the player steps up to and takes like a car. Files: `src/world/drive/boat/` (`handling.js` the physics, `model.js` the hull, `wake.js` the foam, `index.js` the docks and the entries) plus the hooks in `drive.js` (below). Test: `node tools/area.mjs palace tools/boat-test.mjs [--quick]` (CPU, 24 checks; `smolny` proves that an area without the dock has no boat). Not baked (a runtime module): no re-bake needed for the boat itself.

**Where it stands (the spawn table).** `DOCKS` in `drive/boat/index.js` (shared by every area; a boat is built when its dock lies inside the area's play rectangles AND on water, `loadBoats`): `boat` at the Anichkov dock, x -448.9, z -170.3, yaw -0.184 rad (-10.5 deg: the bow points downstream, south, toward the bridge 55 m away, parallel to the quay), the Fontanka on the west side. The water edge on that row is x -452.2, so the hull lies 2.35 m off it (centre 3.3 m); the quay stair (x -452.9, z -166.3) ends at the bow quarter, the tourist pontoon (decoration, no collider) starts 4.7 m north of the stern. An area may carry its own list in `GARAGE.boats` of its area module (`lib/area-tables.js`: `[{ id, x, z, yaw }]`, `[]` = none); without it the shared docks apply. Palace-Liteyny has the boat, Smolninskoye (territory far to the east) none; the Admiralty and Vladimirsky areas get it automatically if their territory contains the dock.

**Taking it.** The interaction layer offers "[E] Сесть в катер · Get in the motor boat" within 5.6 m of the camera (the hull centre, the same nearest-wins rule as the cars), so from the water edge of the quay stair, wading beside it, or from the stair's lower landing. `drive.enter('boat')`; `drive.exit()` (E, below 25 km/h): the helmsman is set down beside the hull on the water bed (wading, as in 13.7) or on a landing, never inside the hull and never on a quay that is 3 m above the boat; the boat floats where it was left (a solid box again) and can be taken again. The car code paths are unchanged: `drive.fleet` is still the four cars (the boats are `drive.boats`, `drive.vehicles` = both), `drive.vehicle.kind === 'boat'`, `drive.car.floats === true` while it is driven.

**Controls** (the car's, `drive.js readInput`, touch included): W / Up throttle, S / Down brake then slow reverse, A D / Left Right steer (swapped when backing up like the car), Space the emergency stop (the handbrake), G horn, V camera (chase / high / cockpit: the helmsman's eye), E get out, R respawn. Touch: the left stick / wheel and the pedals as for a car; NITRO / HOP / FLIP do nothing for a boat (the HUD hides the nitro bar and the body gauge and shows its own key line, `hud.setProfile`).

**Physics** (`drive/boat/handling.js`, the table `BOAT_TUNING`; the interface of `createHandling`: the same state fields, `step(dt, input)`, `reset`, `events`, `carCenter`, so the HUD, the camera, the sound, the hand-off and the touch layer read it unchanged).
* It FLOATS: `car.y` is `physics.waterLevel(x, z)` (eased 10 /s over a change of level where two waterways meet), the body trims bow-up under power (planing), squats and dips with the acceleration and heels INTO a turn; the model rocks by itself (a slow heave, roll and pitch in `model.set`). A cell without water (`waterLevel` null) is the shore: the boat may not be there (nine probes of the hull, 0.4 m outside it, so the quay railings beyond the water edge are never touched); a move into it is refused axis by axis (the boat slides along the bank and loses speed), a boat aground can move out; "the shallows" slow it from 4-6 m before the bank when dry land lies ahead (extra drag 1.6 /s), so a run at 45 km/h ends 0.4 m from the quay with no hit event, no damage, no teleport.
* Thrust 6.2 m/s2 fading toward 17.5 m/s with a linear hull drag 0.2 /s: about 46-48 km/h at full throttle after 10 s, a long coast; S brakes at 7.5 m/s2 then reverses to ~12 km/h; the rudder needs flow (speed > 4.5 m/s or throttle), a turn has inertia (yaw rate 1.05 rad/s at full lock, less at speed); the keel is weak: the sideways speed dies at 1.7 /s (0.7 with Space), so it slides in a turn (about 2-4 m/s of slide at 45 km/h).
* Collisions: `drive/collision.js createCarCollisions` unchanged: the same solids, impulses and knock system as a car. The collision height `SPEC_BOAT.hgt` is the hull only (0.95 m over the water): the Anichkov deck passes over it (clearance under the middle arch 3.7 m over the hull; the windshield tops out at 1.34 m), the piers, abutments, pedestals, fallen furniture and anything standing in the water stop it with a `hit` (thump, shake, no damage: `boat.damage` is an empty state of the damage shape). The two PIERS of the Anichkov Bridge got colliders for this (`landmarksC/bridge.js bridgeColliders`: a 3.6 m box the width of the bridge and a round cutwater at each end, from y -8 to -0.5: nothing on the deck is touched); the three arches stay open.
